# Тема: иерархия

Дословные слова авторов по теме «иерархия»: 286 фрагментов из 111 роликов. Читай подряд — это опыт практиков в их собственных словах; «Мысль» и «Для Напарника» — вывод разметчика.



# 🎬 30% Conversion Rate - PPC Expert Reveals The Only Landing Page Design You Need [Real Live Page] — Michael Nadalin | Google Ads & Paid Ads Lead Gen


### Простой первый экран: заголовок, логотип, форма; 80% не листают дальше  ·  [01:29](https://youtu.be/vtajfv7_QFo?t=89)
**Мысль:** При рекламном трафике цель — действие сразу; считается, что 80% не покидают hero, поэтому всё нужное там.

> So, as we get to the landing page, it's really simple from the start. We've got a very clear headline with a logo and then a form or a call to action here. Now, the reason why we do this is to keep it as simple as possible. If someone's coming from an ad, we've gone after very high intent keywords on Google or they've seen our meta ads and we've got a very clear message there. We want to get them to the page and for them to take action as fast as possible. We don't want them to spend much time on the page. So, we ideally want them to take action immediately. Now, on this landing page, as we scroll down, you'll start to see there's a lot more information, which is crucial. But statistics show that 80% of people will not leave the hero section or the header section, which is this section here. So, our goal will be for people to read the headline, read the sub headline, read the headline on the form, fill out the form, and submit it.

**Для Напарника:** Первый экран лендинга содержит обещание и один CTA; остальное — для тех, кто хочет изучить.



# 🎬 4 самых важных аспекта композиции в UX UI дизайне — UPROCK


### Симметрия успокаивает, а визуальный вес определяется опасностью: движение и контраст  ·  [00:36](https://youtu.be/RXlAyFbamh0?t=36)
**Мысль:** Мозг присваивает больший вес контрастному и подвижному объекту, и любой крупный вес можно уравновесить мелкими. Аккуратно сбалансированный контент не вызывает ощущение, что тебя бросает из стороны в сторону.

> Humans inherently love symmetry. Well, that's just how we are wired. If we are in a cave and one of our tribesmen has some weird look on his face, it means he is sick and might be dangerous to us . Therefore, the more symmetrical my cave-dwelling colleague’s face is, the safer and more attractive he seems to me. Right. So, globally, symmetry gives us a sense of calm. And accordingly, if you conduct research, you'll find that when content— information, text, photos—is neatly and symmetrically balanced, it’s more comfortable for us to process that information. We don't feel tossed around from one side to the other. Right. And the world is set up in such a way that, actually, symmetry isn't the most frequent phenomenon, even though it surrounds us everywhere. The sun, you go outside, you see the elite of people, as we’ve already determined, and, accordingly, everything is plus or minus symmetrical . But sometimes, especially in design, we don't have the option to place everything symmetrically. And that is where balance comes to our aid. Balance is directly built through visual weight . Here we have a small one; we can immediately understand that this is a small visual weight, and this is a large visual weight. Based on what? Because this one is more dangerous. So, the more dangerous an object is—that is, if it moves, is more contrasty, or stands out—the more visually accented it is for us, and the more our brain assigns it maximum visual weight. And directly, if we talk about it in terms of information, any visual weight, a large object, can be balanced out by smaller ones.

**Для Напарника:** На Фокусе уравновешивать тяжёлый таймер лёгкими деталями, а двигающийся элемент использовать только для события.


### Пользователь сканирует в поисках якорей; дизайнер строит траекторию взгляда  ·  [03:46](https://youtu.be/RXlAyFbamh0?t=226)
**Мысль:** Задача дизайнера в том, чтобы человек получал максимум за минимум энергии. Он не читает, а ищет якоря, поэтому нужна понятная линия сверху вниз.

> Globally, the designer's task is to create a design that meets the user's need to gain the maximum while spending the minimum amount of energy. And how can we achieve that? By default, the user scans your design. What does that mean? They aren't reading; they are looking in a unfocused way, searching for anchors. And they still have a certain point around which they direct their focus. It focuses like this—when they see something interesting, they click and focus, and they can move it around like this, here, there, however they want. See? And our task is not to strain the person's imagination, but to make a clear, hyper-logical line of direction—the optimal trajectory for the gaze.

**Для Напарника:** Экран Фокуса строить как одну вертикальную линию взгляда с якорями: шаг, таймер, действие.


### Две точки задают линию: края и углы блоков создают скрытые направляющие  ·  [06:08](https://youtu.be/RXlAyFbamh0?t=368)
**Мысль:** Не нужно рисовать линии или стрелки: выровненные края текстовых и фото-блоков подсказывают траекторию, а заголовки подтверждают её.

> The human brain is such a creature that you only need to give it two points, and it immediately draws a line. You don't need to literally draw lines. You need to provide hints of these lines. And the person goes: "Oh, a line, I want to run along it." We have a lot of automatisms. This is one of them. And we are those damn manipulators who must guide the user through our content in a way that it happens to them quite casually. A little walk through our actually super uninteresting content. And, accordingly, how do we build these lines? First of all, look, if we are talking about points, then it is the text area, for example, the corners of these areas—it is either a photo area where there is a photo or video, or the text itself. The edges of content areas —that is, if we have a photo, then this edge, or the left or right edge, or the top, these all align like lines, as well as the central axes of the areas. Let's take a look together. Do you see this corner? It literally says: "I am a corner of this.""Could you walk along me right here?" I am like: " Bam-bam-bam." See, here, so I already see that I have a motorcycle, I have a line. Notice how cleverly this is all put together. I mean, as a user, I don’t consciously know it, but I immediately grasp that there’s some kind of implicit line here. We don't need to literally draw a line or add an arrow saying "read here." We just need to provide a hint.

**Для Напарника:** Выравнивать левые края блоков Дома по одной вертикали, чтобы взгляд шёл сам.


### Визуальная логика: вложенность по размеру показывает главное и придаток  ·  [11:29](https://youtu.be/RXlAyFbamh0?t=689)
**Мысль:** Иерархическая система акцентов объясняет, что важнее и что к чему относится. Вес нужен и для баланса, и для понимания приоритетов.

> And the fourth aspect of composition is visual logic. Our task is to globally build visual logic in the layout. That is, a hierarchical system of accents. Look, there is an important thing, and there is a less important one. I understand that it belongs to it, that is, it is nested, because this one is bigger and this one is smaller. Here, Transalfa, I understand it is more important than "about our production." There. Or here, these "caps." I understand that this is the most important, and this is at the second level, despite the fact that this is closer to here. Between these two, I understand that this is more important, and this is its appendage. And in this way, we solve any question. This is the most important, and this is an appendage to it. This is the most important, and this is like a part of it. And with the help of this weight, we create not only what we discussed at the beginning of the lecture—that visual weight is needed to form a balance—but you must also maintain that balance while making it clear to the user what is important and what is less important.

**Для Напарника:** Группа «шаг и его действие» должна читаться как главный элемент с придатком, а не как два равных блока.



# 🎬 5 SaaS UI/UX mistakes that SCREAM you Vibe Code — Kole Jain


### Не отдавай ИИ раскладку: дублирование блоков и лишние ссылки  ·  [01:09](https://youtu.be/PDcQJOPby1k?t=69)
**Мысль:** ИИ дублирует одни и те же KPI на разных экранах и перегружает сайдбар второстепенными ссылками. Пользователь ищет данные там, где они логично должны быть.

> These four KPIs at the top show up not once, not twice, but three times in a small app. Bringing me to my next point. Never let an AI choose your layout either. Starting with the sidebar, if I'm in custom domains or teams, the amount of clicks and links doesn't really matter to me. If I need that information, I'll visit the dashboard or the analytics tab, which we definitely need to add. We'll align this to the left and tighten up the spacing, too.

**Для Напарника:** Не повторять один и тот же счётчик (число сессий) на Доме, Фокусе и Мире без причины.


### Разгрузка перегруженных карточек: меню действий, иконки вместо чипов  ·  [01:41](https://youtu.be/PDcQJOPby1k?t=101)
**Мысль:** Кнопки сворачиваются в меню, чипы заменяются иконками, ключевые данные выравниваются по краям.

> Gradient profile circles with letters in them. So, let's swap this for an account card. And with that, we can get rid of these links and tuck them into a popover on click and collapse settings and billing and usage together. And finally for these cards, they're feeling a little busy. Let's collapse these buttons into a triple dot menu. Move the date to the center. Collapse these chips to just icons. And move clicks to the right.

**Для Напарника:** Карточки шагов и reward держать лёгкими: главное действие видно, второстепенные уходят в меню.


### Тарифы: меньше планов, крупная цена, показать скидку и что даёт следующий план  ·  [03:53](https://youtu.be/PDcQJOPby1k?t=233)
**Мысль:** Пять планов слишком много, название тарифа не важно, а цена в месяц важна. Реальную скидку и дополнительные возможности нужно показывать явно.

> Also, having five plans is really not ideal. Hobby probably makes the most sense to drop, but at least one of them needs to go. The hierarchy isn't bad, but I think we can tune it up a little with that extra width we have. Let's drop some of the unnecessary elements and adjust the colors. Drop the size of the name since no one cares about that. Increase the size of the cost per month since people do care about that. Business has become enterprise since if you need 50,000 links per month, you probably need more. Showing the actual discount the user is getting is very important. And also showing them what the next plan includes that the current plan doesn't have.

**Для Напарника:** Если появятся тарифы, оставить 2–3 плана, цену сделать крупной и не давить скидкой, соблюдая принцип без стыда.



# 🎬 7 UI/UX mistakes that SCREAM you’re a beginner — Kole Jain

**Мысль:** Стрелка при возможности свайпа и обводки без необходимости только визуальный шум.

Looking at this screen, we can get rid of this arrow since we'll be able to swipe on mobile. And same idea down below. We can also get rid of this arrow since it's really just visual clutter and dim down the color of the button.

**Для Напарника:** Убрать декоративные стрелки там, где работает drag-to-reply и свайп, оставив действие очевидным без украшения.


### Графики не надо переоформлять: ось и читаемые столбцы важнее эстетики  ·  [06:27](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=387)
**Мысль:** Закруглённые столбцы без оси и 16 столбцов на 7 дней нечитаемы, а простой график даёт больше пользы.

> Charts are so often messed up by overdesigning the thing that I thought I should make a point to mention it. I have two charts here. Which one do you think I got from Dribble? The one on the right. Yes. And it's basically impossible to read without any vertical axis. And even if we had a vertical axis, it's hard to tell where the bar ends with a rounded top. And then there are 16 bars here, but only 7 days of the week. Why would you do that? Whereas the one on the left is simple, definitely less aesthetic, but conveys so much more useful information to the user.

**Для Напарника:** Если в Мире появится статистика недели, делать её простой и честной, с понятной осью.

# 🎬 [Revealed] The GESTALT Principles Behind Visual Design! | Psychology of UI Design | Design Sundays — Rohan Mishra


### Сходство: ожидания от дизайн-системы и выделение через отличие  ·  [00:10](https://youtu.be/5Up80FUnTVw?t=10)
**Мысль:** Похожее воспринимается как группа с одной функцией, поэтому дизайн-системы создают предсказуемость; отличающийся элемент мгновенно привлекает внимание.

> Let's start with similarity similar objects tend to have same functionality and our mind perceives them as so now when we look at these two markers we know automatically that hey if this marker can write on a whiteboard and this has a thick tip to write then automatically next time I see this in the marker shop I know what this does because they look pretty similar on the contrary if we actually look at two other markers which pretty much do the same job but let's say this one has a smaller tip so now we know that every time I am going to look at a marker like this I know that I can expect a smaller tip to be there if I look at a marker like this I know I can expect a bigger tip to be there today you will see all the big design companies and big design teams using Design Systems that is why we have Design Systems in place because every time if we Define this is how our heading looks or this is how our primary button looks this is how our secondary button looks and we continue to use it across the ecosystem people automatically start expecting that okay this button is supposed to work in a certain way in fact you can actually experiment with similarity you can do the exact opposite because similar items tend to look same and they are tend to be perceived as a part of a group you can actually make something stand out if it's not similar to the rest of the group so then automatically my attention goes to this marker which is not saved to the rest of the lot so make sure to use this law to your convenience and make things stand out in your design.

**Для Напарника:** Главная кнопка отличается от остальных; одинаковые элементы выглядят одинаково по всему приложению.


### Фигура и фон: контраст, размер и размытие для внимания  ·  [01:48](https://youtu.be/5Up80FUnTVw?t=108)
**Мысль:** Выделять можно цветом/контрастом, размером и размытием фона (например, модал подтверждения деструктивных действий).

> It's figure and ground tell me what do you see in this image do you see two people or do you see a horse our mind can very easily distinguish between what is an object versus what is an background and this concept can be beautifully executed throughout our design when you look at a screen and there are various cards versus when you look at a screen where there is a pop-up you can immediately tell where the attention goes so design and Product Industry today is heavily dependent on these pop-ups because they immediately draw users attention to that one area of the screen so how does one really make something stand out there are three ways to do that first is color or contrast when you look at this image you can very easily tell that these two elements are at a different layer or one element is perceived to be in the foreground and one element is perceived to be in the background it's because of the extremely high color contrast between the elements now if there is a low contrast of colors it's very difficult to pick between two items and what is foreground and what is background another way of doing that is size so let's say if there is a small object versus a big object behind that it's very easy to create a contrast the third most important way to create a contrast is blur if I blur out the elements in the background and I keep the sharpness your attention immediately goes to that object and why do you think that's important because sometimes you really want your user to pay attention to certain items for example if you do a destructive action like you're deleting an account or deleting the Devita or wiping off the data of your system you want to have a confirmation pop-up and according to the design of the product they want your attention to be there on the pop-up irrespective of whatever you're doing on the screen all your attention is brought to that pop-up because here the action you take might have an immense impact on your overall experience of the product.

**Для Напарника:** Для модальных моментов (редкая находка, итог сессии) приглушать/размывать фон, чтобы вести внимание.


### Близость: расстояние создаёт группы (заголовок к тексту, поле к подписи)  ·  [03:36](https://youtu.be/5Up80FUnTVw?t=216)
**Мысль:** Чем ближе элементы, тем сильнее они воспринимаются как группа; отсутствие близости заголовка и подписи к полю превращает 2 минуты в 10.

> Let's take a step back and understand how do we even create groups how do we make things look like they are associated to each other well it's all about spacing the closer you place items the more they are tend to be perceived as a group now for instance let's look at the marker example again even if these two markers look very similar and these two markers look different from this group of markers now the way I place them changes everything now you see how two of these tend to look like a group whereas two of these tend to look like a group The closer the things are the more they are tend to be in a group whereas now if I separate them away and make these two in a group you will see suddenly the group has changed pacing pauses or gaps changes everything now let's look at some great examples in our UI when you look at a heading and when you look at a body content if they are further away from each other do you think you can perceive them as one group where do you think this heading belongs where do you think this text belong do you think it belongs to the group above it or do you think it belongs to the content below it hmm that's difficult right but let's say we fix the space station let's say we bring it closer to the contents that are below now suddenly they look like a group how close you place items to each other are tend to be perceived as a group for instance if I'm designing a form if the input Fields versus the titles are not placed close to each other this can be the exact difference between does it take me two minutes to fill a form or does it take me 10 minutes to fill a form now in this competitive world we do not want our users to spend so much time on small tasks because what is stopping them from going to our competitors so let's save their time as much as we can.

**Для Напарника:** Подписи и заголовки прижимать к своему содержимому; отдельные блоки разводить отступом (как разделитель между переключателями на Фокусе).


### Общая область (карточки) сильнее сходства и близости  ·  [07:15](https://youtu.be/5Up80FUnTVw?t=435)
**Мысль:** Фон, граница или тень делают набор элементов группой, даже разных по виду; на странице цен выделенная карточка плана выбивается.

> We tend to see elements placed in the same container as a group what's more interesting is we tend to assume that these elements will have the same character or function that is why carpet plays an important role in the interior design industry today interior designers use Carpets to Define spaces and to Define what the space is meant to do for example a coffee table and a sofa will be put under one rug that signifies that this is a space where once you enter you can sit and have coffee here you can find some similar examples in the UI industry today designers are using the slot to its maximum Advantage by applying borders by giving a background color or even using Shadows do you think you have seen this example somewhere I'm pretty sure you're thinking of cards yes that is right all the cards that we see today in fact not just cards it's the tiles and even in tables that we see today we are seeing the law of common region being used every time you place multiple elements on a card this seemed to be a part of a group and let's say you are a product company who wants to show their features you're going to put all of the different features which might have different colors which might have different size which might have different orientations once you put them all on the same card they suddenly become a part of a group in fact the most interesting fact about this is it overwrite similarity and it overrides proximity so even if you have two elements placed closer to each other that can be perceived as group and if you divide it into two sets and put them under different backgrounds they will actually be perceived as two separate groups that was not the case with proximity in fact go ahead and try this experiment so if you can see in this example there are different kinds of objects and they can be classified in a very different manner using similarity but now let's put a background behind it and see what happens voila reduce see the magic suddenly this group stands out even if it has a completely different set of objects and that's how you can use your designs to show visual hierarchy and how a user should perceive the groups in fact can you think of a great example of this within your designs dashboards is the way to go if you have seen dashboards you can totally tell there are different kinds of graphs and charts and there is a lot of information that needs to be reflected together the best way to group that information is using a common region the minute you give a background outline or a shadow behind it immediately becomes a group and now you know how to chunk your information and how the user is going to see them and the more you look at these day-to-day examples in your favorite apps and in your favorite websites the more you're going to remember these laws in fact one of the best examples of commentation is the pricing plans page you can really see how adding a shadow or an outline to one particular pricing plan really makes it stand out from the rest of them.

**Для Напарника:** Карточки на Доме и Мире задавать общим фоном; на странице цен один план выделить обводкой/тенью.



# 🎬 A History and Future of AR and VR | Amber Case | AR in Action — AR in ACTION
