# 🎬 A Lesson in How Inconsistent UI Kills the First Experience | App Breakdown #88 — App Breakdown


### Главная кнопка всегда в одном месте  ·  [08:28](https://youtu.be/hgpk0ejGs-E?t=508)
**Мысль:** Когда CTA прыгает с экрана на экран, нарушается моторная память и растёт когнитивная нагрузка.

> Notice how the CTAs moved from the middle of the screen to the bottom and then other screens are in different places. Try to always keep the CTA in the same place.

**Для Напарника:** В Фокусе и онбординге главный CTA в одной зоне у большого пальца (нижняя треть), на одинаковой высоте на всех шагах.


### Пульсирует, но не нажимается: ложный сигнал аффорданса  ·  [11:02](https://youtu.be/hgpk0ejGs-E?t=662)
**Мысль:** Анимация подсказывает «нажми», а ничего не происходит — это ломает доверие к интерфейсу.

> If I tap that, nothing happens. So, this looks like the most tappable thing ever and it's pulsing, but it's not tappable.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает диегетичность из CLAUDE.md: пульсировать/двигаться должно только то, что реально интерактивно или реально живёт в сцене. Проверить, что декоративные движения не выглядят кнопками.



# 🎬 Accessible Web Design: What Is It & How To Do It — Flux Academy


### Дизайнеры работают на хороших экранах и делают иерархию серыми оттенками и мелким шрифтом  ·  [04:11](https://youtu.be/-ao_Kc_8rpE?t=251)
**Мысль:** Люди с плохим зрением или на слабых экранах не могут читать низкоконтрастный и мелкий текст; как в упаковке лекарств, должен быть минимум сочетания цвета и размера.

> So the first concept is color contrast and text size and the idea here is that as I said some people may be old people or maybe people that have some in parodies can't see as good and maybe you know we're designers will usually work maybe on a Mac or on a fancy on a fancy display that shows color really nice and so we like to create hierarchies with like let's say some kind of a shade of grey or something but for people who can't see super well or have an amazing display like we have that makes reading really a tough job sometimes to the point of impossible the same size the same thing goes for text size we as designers we like to make things fancy and leave a lot of kind of like white space and stuff like that by using tiny fonts but we have to consider that for a lot of people using these type of super small fonts makes it hard to impossible to read so just like that in packaging design when somebody is you know designing and medication and they put this tiny little paper there's actually laws that says the font size can't be lower than something because people old people need to be able to read this the same thing goes with web site guidelines your font can't be below some combination of color and size to make sure that it will be seen.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает требование контраста muted-foreground и минимального размера шрифта; иерархию строить размером и весом, а не только серым оттенком.


### Не полагаться только на цвет для различия состояний  ·  [06:14](https://youtu.be/-ao_Kc_8rpE?t=374)
**Мысль:** Люди с дальтонизмом не отличат жёлтую и оранжевую метки; статус нужно дублировать текстом или формой.

> Now one more thing that you should when you're designing take in mind is that you don't want to use color alone to make kind of difference differentiations bright because some people are color blind and they might not see the difference so let me give you an example so let's say here if you look at the right we have two types of card and one of them is labeled with a yellow and one is labeled with an orange but somebody that has colorblindness might not actually see that they are different and if your imagine that you're designing some kind of maybe a task management or something like that and you want to show a status of a project if you're gonna be only relying on color then somebody that's looking at it might not even see that there's a difference or urgency or something like that so make sure that when you're designing you add something else to this besides color for example if you look at the example on the right I've added for example team egg and Team B so it's not only relying on color obviously for those of us who can see well we're gonna be able to make decisions or understand very quickly because of the help of color but color is not the only way.

**Для Напарника:** Статусы шагов и чек-ина обозначать иконкой или словом, а не только оттенком.



# 🎬 Agent Output Is Not UX: Rendering Layer Your LLM Pipeline Is Missing - Bala Ramdoss, Amazon Lens — AI Engineer


### Ответ модели не равен интерфейсу: бронь за пару тапов вместо абзаца  ·  [00:01](https://youtu.be/maTp79FD9gI?t=1)
**Мысль:** Тот же ответ модели, показанный как дата, время и пара тапов, доводит до действия; текстовый ответ оставляет работу человеку.

> I asked my AI assistant to help me reserve a table at a popular restaurant. Here's what it gave me. Now, it's not wrong. The phone number is there. The hours are right. It even knows the walk-in oyster bar at the front. The model did the real work, but look at the outcome. I have to do my research and work towards actually booking the table. Like, we've been here for a while. Now, imagine if you were to build something like this for your customers. What would you want it to do? Same answer, rendered like this instead. A date, a time, a couple of taps, and then you're done. The agentic product you're building already has all the tools to support your customer's needs. The only thing that you need to focus on is the layer between the model and something that a human can interact with.

**Для Напарника:** Ответы AI-дробления (api/breakdown) показывать карточками шагов с кнопкой «Начать», а не простынёй текста.



# 🎬 Alex Hormozi's Advice on Landing Pages — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Главный рычаг — первый экран: заголовок и картинка, 60% не скроллят  ·  [05:42](https://youtu.be/CNRiNz0YYlM?t=342)
**Мысль:** Крупнейшие скачки конверсии (до +130%) дают тесты above the fold. Это единственная часть страницы, которую гарантированно видят 100% людей, а около 60% не скролят дальше.

> I'll give you the two biggest things that we look at. The first thing, this sounds obvious and simple, but people don't do it, is we simply split test the headlines on our landing pages. That's it. The next thing that we'll split test is going to be the image that is underneath of the headline. Because what we've seen from the vast majority of all of the tests that we've done is that almost all of the juice that's going to come, the biggest jumps in conversion rate happen above the fold. Testing the above the fold has historically gotten us the best ROI as well. And we've seen conversion increases by upwards of 130% by testing the above the fold. And we've seen this across both our B2B clients and our e-commerce clients as well. The above the fold is the topmost area of any page. It's the first thing that people will see. And there's two things that you need to understand when it comes to the above the fold. The first thing is that the above the fold is the only section on any page that you can guarantee with certainty that 100% of people will see. The second thing is that statistically 60% of people will never scroll past the above the fold section. That is why, as Alex suggests, the above the fold is the most critical area to test and it's where most people should be spending 99% of their efforts optimizing in the beginning stages.

**Для Напарника:** На лендинге Напарника весь смысл и CTA должны жить на первом экране мобильного (390×844). Самые дорогие правки — заголовок и главный визуал (кот/остров).



# 🎬 Alex Hormozi's Landing Page Strategy for 2026 — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Hero-картинка должна визуально доказывать обещание заголовка  ·  [05:48](https://youtu.be/zA0B-VwOPn4?t=348)
**Мысль:** Изображение показывает результат, который обещан текстом, и тема результата повторяется по всей странице.

> So, the next thing Alex says is that your hero image should also visually represent your dream outcome. Most landing pages suck. Here's how to have them suck less. The hero image should add proof to the headline and subheadline. So, either I'm showing you the thing you're going to get that or the outcome that you want to experience. >> If your headline is promising a luxury high-end closet, your image should actually show that. If it's promising a straighter, brighter smile, I should see that in your image. If it's promising smoother, brighter skin, again, I should see that visually. The dream outcome should not only be in the above the fold, but it should also be throughout your entire landing page in the headlines and imagery.

**Для Напарника:** Hero: остров, который вырос, и кот рядом — как визуальное доказательство обещания «прогресс виден».



# 🎬 Amazon App Critique: Product Design Mock Interview (ft. Google Designer) — Aced (formerly Exponent)


### Нет иерархии на главной и эмоциональный провал «Дня отца»  ·  [03:19](https://youtu.be/FBzdzRxrReQ?t=199)
**Мысль:** Много разрывов без ясной подписи, что именно ты видишь; контент «ко Дню отца» может ранить того, у кого нет отца. Контекстный контент рискует задеть человека.

> There's not real a clear information hierarchy around this so like in terms of navigating the page is a little dizzying because there's a lot of breaks in the page and then like those breaks aren't very clear of like what actually is the thing i'm looking at so like am i seeing father's day like what if i never i never had a father you know there's things that potentially like here could break the experience for a user.

**Для Напарника:** Реплики Напарника и сезонные/контекстные предложения должны быть нейтральными и не предполагать семейную ситуацию; тон без вины (CLAUDE.md §1).


### Тонкая типографика заголовков растворяется в фоне  ·  [07:44](https://youtu.be/FBzdzRxrReQ?t=464)
**Мысль:** Тонкие заголовки сливаются с фоном и ослабляют иерархию; человек опирается на название, а не только на картинку, чтобы решить тапнуть.

> Here so i would like try to make these more bold like they're very like thin typography choices so like having like something like what do you think of this item but have that applied to some of these titles to add more clarity around hierarchy because to me it kind of blends into the background and i'm assuming they're putting more emphasis on the images than the titles but at the same time like i'm not just going to look at images and probably or as a user you know you wouldn't probably look at images and be like i'm going to tap this you kind of want to see why you're seeing that specific thing so if you missed the titling it's kind of important in terms of actually making that purchase.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять вес и контраст заголовков карточек и кнопок на тёмном фоне Напарника; корректировать вес, а не светлоту фона (CLAUDE.md §3).


### Должно быть ясно, что тапабельно, а что нет  ·  [14:14](https://youtu.be/FBzdzRxrReQ?t=854)
**Мысль:** Разные типы кнопок и «псевдокнопки» без различимого смысла (see all vs see more) путают, что произойдёт при нажатии.

> Like i think there's obviously like we have some issues around like they have like many different types of buttons so like we have the secondary button where it says see more but then we also have like these other like sort of pseudo buttons here which i'm not sure if these are actions or like or like are they ctas like i'm not really sure like what that distinct distinction is so like as a designer to me like there's no clear understanding of like what's tappable and what's not in terms of like is this a button or is this like going to bring me to a separate page is this going to expand the page i think that's super important.

**Для Напарника:** Единый язык кнопок: основное действие (Play) и «следующий шаг» (ArrowRight) уже различаются иконкой; не плодить промежуточных стилей.


### Три тапа до нужной информации: глубина навигации убивает сканирование  ·  [15:34](https://youtu.be/FBzdzRxrReQ?t=934)
**Мысль:** Чтобы увидеть несколько заказов, нужно идти на два уровня вглубь; быстрое сканирование невозможно.

> Business model like i have to like tap to see more and then i only see like one of the items or i've never seen like a bunch of the items but like potentially have to go to a separate page and then another page actually see more data to that item so it's kind of like three taps so just to kind of see that information.

**Для Напарника:** Ключевые данные (текущий шаг, остаток таймера, ориентир) держать на поверхности экрана, не глубже одного тапа.



# 🎬 App Critique: Calm (A UX Designer's Perspective) — paintpops


### Экраны открытия для новичков вредят постоянным: фильтры спрятаны, длинная горизонтальная прокрутка  ·  [04:07](https://youtu.be/DK4PsSlR36k?t=247)
**Мысль:** Красивые карточки хороши для первого знакомства, но возвращающемуся человеку приходится прокручивать каждый раз; длинное горизонтальное меню создаёт трение, лучше вертикальное.

> Are the filters here that says by narrator and all sleep stories i understand that having the subcategories with pretty cards up top is great for someone who's new to the app and who's you know just discovering this for the very first time but if i'm a regular user and i'm past the whole discovery phase it would be kind of annoying to have to scroll all the way down each time just to see these filters all right next moving on to the meditate section so this is what i use most often and honestly i mostly use the navigation up top to filter by category and again this main meditate screen seems more for people who are just discovering what the app has to offer versus like regular users for that reason i wish that this top level navigation wasn't a horizontal scroll because it contains so many choices if someone wanted to regularly listen to meditations on personal growth they would have to scroll all the way right to it each time which causes a lot of friction in the ux maybe if it were only a few menu items the horizontal scroll might be okay but when the list is this long and this lengthy i think i prefer to have it displayed vertically.

**Для Напарника:** Для частых действий в Доме (чек-ин, продолжить шаг) не прятать за карточками открытия; поднимать выше с ростом опыта.


### Готовые подборки как учебный план, плюс фильтр по длительности  ·  [05:42](https://youtu.be/DK4PsSlR36k?t=342)
**Мысль:** Группированный контент даёт ощущение учебной программы, но людям нужен фильтр по ведущему и времени (например, есть только 15 минут).

> However i do like that when i click into them i can see that they've already curated a set of meditations for me chunking content is super helpful and allows users to have kind of like a curriculum based experience that being said i do wish there was some way for me to just filter by narrator or duration kind of like with workouts there are probably some instructors you gel with more and you know maybe you only have 15 minutes in your schedule.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус: выбор длительности привязан к реально свободному времени человека; подборки шагов сгруппированы.



# 🎬 App Onboarding Best Practices: Why Users Quit Before They Start — Kris Greenwald


### Эксперимент с джемами: 6 вариантов дают в 10 раз лучшую конверсию, чем 24  ·  [03:43](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=223)
**Мысль:** Большая выкладка привлекает больше людей (60% против 40%), но покупают 3% против 30%; избыток выбора снижает действие.

> We can start with the paradox of choice. There's a really interesting study back in 2000 and an upscale grocery store in California where a tasty booth was set up. Some hours this researcher put out 24 jams. Other hours they put out six. And you know what they put very interesting. The big 24 jar display was better at pulling people in. So, 60% of shoppers who walked past 24 jams stopped, whereas only 40% stopped for six jams. Now, stopping is one thing, but we want to make money. So, of those who stopped, only 3% actually bought something at the 24 jam display. But, this part's super interesting. At the six-jar display, 30% purchased a jar. So, that is 50% less traffic, but 10 times better conversion.

**Для Напарника:** Выбор длительности и шага ограничивать несколькими пунктами; не показывать десяток вариантов на старте (закон Хика).


### Умный дефолт превращает открытый вопрос в «да или нет»  ·  [06:10](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=370)
**Мысль:** Плитки рекомендаций (80% просмотров Netflix идут из рекомендаций) заменяют поиск и выбор лёгким согласием.

> I say open Spotify, or in my case, YouTube Music. I don't open this app, search for a song, listen to it, then search for another one, listen to that. Almost nobody does. I tap one of the first tiles on the screen, maybe my liked songs, supermix, something built for what I've already played as a recommendation. Netflix started learning from its initial indecisions and published a paper on their own recommender system. About 80% of hours streamed comes from recommendations now, and like 20% from the search. So, that 80/20 in favor of not deciding, but going with a recommendation. So, what those tiles do is convert an open-ended question into a yes or no. And a yes or a no is mentally cheap on your user. That is your dominant option and the thing that makes choice overload disappear. We call this strategy smart default.

**Для Напарника:** Дом предлагает одну готовую рекомендацию («25 минут, начнём с…») с возможностью поменять, вместо пустого выбора.


### Дефолт воспринимается как совет: автозаписи подняли участие с 37% до 86%  ·  [07:06](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=426)
**Мысль:** При автозаписи 61% остались на значениях по умолчанию, тогда как среди выбиравших сами тот же вариант взяли 1%; предзаполненное значение читается как социальный сигнал нормы.

> There's a really interesting study on 401(k)s, and this company changed theirs from an opt-in to an automatic enrollment. And from that alone, participation went up from 37% to 86%. But, a statistic that's more useful for us is that the plan had default contribution rates and a default fund. And 61% of the auto-enrolled employees stayed on both. They just accepted those smart defaults. What's interesting about this is that among the employees who had joined the old way, so they opted in themselves and chose for themselves, only about 1% picked that same combination. So, did you catch that? With a smart default, user behavior changed from selecting a choice 1% of the time to 61% of the time. That default didn't just influence a decision, it was the decision. So now, why would that happen? People don't read a default as a placeholder. They read it as advice. Employees describe taking the default as investment guidance. So, a pre-filled value is a social signal. It says, "This is what people like you choose." And we as a people rate that heavily because copying everyone else has been excellent survival strategy for most of human history. So, when you pre-select two guests and 7:00 p.m. for your restaurant, you're not just saving a few taps. You're saying, "This is normal. You're in the right place. You don't have to think about this one.".

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает Default Effect из §4: метка «рекомендуем» на 25 минутах, пока человек сам не изменил выбор.


### Дефолт честный, если это то, что человек выбрал бы сам  ·  [08:36](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=516)
**Мысль:** Умный дефолт это самое частое значение, а не самое выгодное бизнесу; иначе он превращается в манипуляцию.

> Responsibility dilemma here. Because a default is smart when it is the option that the user would most likely have picked anyway. But it's sleazy when it's the option that only you would have picked for them because it's most advantageous for you and maybe even detrimental to the user opposed to other choices. So, pre-select the most common value, not the most profitable one.

**Для Напарника:** Дефолты (длительность, время напоминания) выбирать по реальной статистике пользы для человека, а не ради удержания.


### Готовые варианты вместо свободного ввода: утро/день/вечер, рекомендованные дни, план до регистрации  ·  [13:36](https://youtu.be/lMOMplCvfZA?t=816)
**Мысль:** Выбор из готовых вариантов легче свободной формы; время суток вместо часов уменьшает нагрузку; выдача недельного плана до входа включает закон взаимности.

> This is much easier to get me continuing. So, I get consistent and then which days work best. So, I'm recommended to run Tuesday, Thursday, Saturday. That seems reasonable. I don't really have to think about my whole schedule and everything. I kind of go with this recommendation. If I truly want to switch some things up, maybe Monday, Wednesday, Friday, it's much easier for me to do that with this kind of selectable interface than maybe most would be. And then, what time do you usually have free? So, instead of giving me all the hours in the day and everything that I could do, all the different time increments and everything, it just gives me morning, afternoon, and evening. Around 7:00, around 12:00, around 6:30. Very easy, very welcoming, and I can just select one of these. I'd like to run in the morning. That's easy. And now we can see I already have some value from this. I have a whole week one plan. So, I can get started without even needing to sign into this application yet. That makes me much more likely to want to use this software because of the law of reciprocity when it comes to humans. So, once we get something, we want to give something. That's how we work.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-ин: «когда обычно легче начать?» с тремя чипами (утро, день, вечер) и дефолтом; сразу показать что-то ценное в ответ.



# 🎬 ART/ARCHITECTURE – Dieter Rams — The School of Life


### Простота это достижение: твёрдая ясность о том, что важно (Дитер Рамс)  ·  [01:11](https://youtu.be/nXwpn90Gdec?t=71)
**Мысль:** Рамс сделал выключатель частью регулятора громкости, выкинув лишнее; для простоты нужна уверенность и ясность приоритетов.

> Rams wanted to reduce everything back to just a few things that matter most you can see this in one of his early products the PC3 SV radio there were many more things which could be done with a radio one could add in bass antenna control separate am and FM dials an alarm clock an output cord for larger speakers and so on but Rams pushed in the opposite direction he made the onoff switch a part of the volume control which hadn't been done before and which people argued against but the end result is deeply satisfying you have to be confident to dare to be simple with radios or in other areas of life for example in an expensive restaurant there's pressure to order something elaborate even though deep down we might actually just feel like ordering cheese on toast being simple can make you vulnerable but Simplicity is really an achievement it follows from a hard one clarity about what matters.

**Для Напарника:** Каждый экран Напарника: одно главное действие, всё прочее убрать или скрыть.



# 🎬 Best Practices for Reducing Extraneous Cognitive Load — J.D. Coleman


### Сигналы экономно + когерентность: иллюстрация, а не декорация  ·  [12:27](https://youtu.be/1fEC5rFCATw?t=747)
**Мысль:** Выделение (жирный, подчёркивание) снижает нагрузку, только если применяется редко; всё, что не служит цели (фоновые звуки, лишние картинки), увеличивает нагрузку.

> One of the easiest ways to reduce cognitive load is by signaling provides cues that highlight the organization of essential material thereby reducing extraneous cognitive load throughout the content examples of signaling include bolding underlining highlighting and organizing content into bullets one thing to note is that signaling should be used sparingly to be most defective and yet another best practice in designing to reduce extraneous cognitive load includes the coherence principle designing with the coherence principle in mind includes excluding extraneous words sounds and video everything that you include in your content should align to the established learning objectives remember illustration not decoration this includes background sound non-essential images and any additional treatment that does not somehow aligned to the learning goals.

**Для Напарника:** Согласуется с правилом CLAUDE.md: не добавлять glow/анимации без цели; один акцент на экран.



# 🎬 Calm vs Headspace | Design Lessons for Mindfulness — Pragmatics Studio


### Градиент как намёк на прокрутку не работает, если не очевидно, что можно скроллить  ·  [08:55](https://youtu.be/uJqU4Z2ffic?t=535)
**Мысль:** Хитрое визуальное решение осталось незамеченным: человек не понял, что список прокручивается.

> Whereas if we just quickly scroll through call okay oh actually that wasn't obvious but I thought you were gonna score horizontal honestly yeah it's it's not clear that you can scroll down here they're trying to do this clever thing where they have just like a kind of gradient oh that's not that clear no it's not clear.

**Для Напарника:** Мир: явный признак прокрутки или смещения контента за край экрана.



# 🎬 Character Design Secrets Everyone Should Know — Proko


### Порядок восприятия персонажа: силуэт и пропорции, затем лицо, форма, цвет  ·  [03:52](https://youtu.be/zUATd5Lbo-w?t=232)
**Мысль:** Зритель сначала считывает силуэт, пропорции и масштаб, потом верхнюю часть и лицо, потом язык форм и одежду, в конце цвет и текстуры; из правила бывают исключения.

> In most cases, the design of a character not only starts with a silhouette, proportions and scale. This is where all magic happens and where most challenges arise. When ranked by importance in the design, these elements are usually the first things viewers notice and interpret. Next comes the upper body, especially the head and face. Then the design elements like shape language, gears, weapons, clothing, armor, etc. Lastly, color, textures and patterns come into play. Again, keep in mind that while this is generally the case, there are exceptions. The good example here is the Stormtroopers from Star Wars. I guess in their case the order would probably be this.

**Для Напарника:** Кот и ориентиры должны узнаваться по силуэту даже залитыми одним цветом: проверять читаемость на гряде в тёмном фоне.



# 🎬 Comment RÉUSSIR l'onboarding de vos applications :  ASTUCES et TUTORIEL complet — Lucas & Nathan Simula


### Убери всё, не нужное для главного действия; запрашивай остальное позже  ·  [07:37](https://youtu.be/9QmD5wcHobg?t=457)
**Мысль:** Трение (платёж, данные) выноси как можно дальше по пути, пока у человека нет интереса и ценности (Uber, TikTok).

> Point: you get rid of the rest. It's as simple as that. In fact, everything that isn't useful for carrying out the action, you remove it. It will come later. I'll go back to my examples. Uber, the credit card. Well, I don't remember off the top of my head. If it asks, but if it does ask, you can skip it and then you'll find it again when you pay, actually. So, anything that's a friction point, because payment is actually a friction point. Entering your credit card—everyone has filled an e-commerce cart and then you get to checkout, and you're like "actually no, forget it, it's a no-go." Everyone has done that. Abandoned shopping carts. I don’t have the stats, but it’s huge . Same thing for Uber; you’re hyped, then it’s time to pay. Oh man, that’s annoying. And so, the friction , you want to push it as far back as possible. You see, when they’re desperate, their Uber tells them, " Arriving in 2 minutes." Hurry up, someone else will grab it, just like Booking does so well. And, well, put in your card info, quickly, quickly, quickly! But if you make the onboarding annoying? Oh, forget it. Wait, I don't even know if I’m going to use your app yet. You’re already asking for my money. No, no, that’s not how it works. So add friction, but at the right time, and any info you can ask for later, ask for later. Example, another example, social media apps now. Right? Social media, you go on TikTok, but you can use TikTok without signing up. Actually, after a while, you’ll get a pop-up because they want your data. But anyway, TikTok knows perfectly well: "I’ll get your data; you’ll be so addicted to scrolling that, don't worry, I’ll get everything later." So, add friction— all that useful info for your marketing , for gathering data, or pushing the right content—but do it as late as possible if it’s not mandatory, and remove as much friction as you can.

**Для Напарника:** Не просить ничего, кроме шага задачи; имя и чек-ин предлагать по одному вопросу за визит и после первой пользы.



# 🎬 Config 2024: A modern definition of design craft (Ethan Eissman, SVP Design, Slack) | Figma — Figma


### Иерархия качеств дизайна: функциональность, надёжность, юзабилити, удовольствие  ·  [07:44](https://youtu.be/tNGDgK0aMjA?t=464)
**Мысль:** Наверху пирамиды потребностей стоит «приятно пользоваться», а доступ к ней даёт крафт, но только после надёжной основы.

> Hierarchy of needs right well this is the hierarchy of design qualities at the Baseline the software is functional it works great then it's reliable performant it's free of bugs then it's usable and then finally it's a pleasure to use and the way to think about this Apex T level is by inserting craft into the experience.

**Для Напарника:** Не полировать визуал раньше, чем таймер и сохранение прогресса надёжны.



# 🎬 Dark Mode Website Design | 6 Tips For Better Dark UI Design — WPTuts


### Тёмный UI: не заливай большие области брендовым цветом, держи его как акцент  ·  [00:23](https://youtu.be/JNhG2T6dGOY?t=23)
**Мысль:** Большая цветная заливка утомляет глаза и ухудшает чтение. Спокойный фон ближе к кнопке и акцентный цвет на кнопке и линиях возвращают контраст и вес главного действия.

> So the first thing i want to cover is using brand colors as an accent you've seen in previous ui videos link in the description and in the corner right now if you want to check those out we've kind of got a color palette that i've used and it's this kind of sea foam green alongside some grays and other things so that's our accent color and as you can see down the right hand side i've got a selection of various shades of that accent color and i'll show you why i'm going to use those in a moment at the moment you can see we're using that color for the background of the main area below the image and while it looks nice enough it isn't the best way of working too much of this you start to get a little bit of eye strain it's not the easiest to read so there's already a couple of problems with it so how do we go about rectifying it's very easy let's take this final example as our starting point let's go ahead and set the background inside shirt to the color closer to the button let's select it let's go into our background choose our fill color and we're going to replace this and as you can see immediately that looks considerably better i need to address the sort of highlight key around the outside edge and we'll do that in a moment but obviously by doing this we lose the contrast in the button and this is where our accent color really comes into play let's select our button let's go over to the fill and let's change that to our accent color and you can see immediately things look considerably better let's quickly go and change that outside stroke so all i'm going to do is for now i'm going to remove that completely and you can see we already have a better level of contrast with the text the lines look better the text looks better we need to address one more thing and if i zoom right in you can see we've got this little dark strip that was matching the color we used in the button we need to change that now for our accent color so we'll do the same thing again we'll choose our accent color and we've already created a much better much more professional and cohesive design simply by using our accent color in a much better way it now has more visual weight and impact the buy now button is a lot more evident and everything just ties together a little better.

**Для Напарника:** Тёплый акцент Напарника использовать только на кнопке старта и мелких деталях, а большие поверхности оставить приглушёнными.


### Не используй чистый белый и чистый чёрный: оттенки фона и светло-серый текст  ·  [03:59](https://youtu.be/JNhG2T6dGOY?t=239)
**Мысль:** Чистый белый на тёмном резок для глаз, чистый чёрный слишком контрастен. Лучший компромисс это светлее или темнее оттенки самого фона и текст светло-серого.

> However that doesn't really work it's okay in small amounts but when you've got a design that uses a lot of the same kind of layout it can get a little bit harsh on your eyes in the same way that we talked about with using the accent color so what i would always recommend is stay away from doing that and you're much better off using a lighter shade or a darker shade of the actual background you can see by using the same color light that we used in the previous one with the lighter shade of the background color for this particular card design you can see things are a lot more coherent avoiding that kind of strong contrast that we have working on the left hand side you may also be thinking maybe i could go for total black to give us a darker shade over what we've got inside the background well that doesn't really work too well either here's a third example where you can see black has been set as the background and in this example the contrast is a little too much in the opposite direction so out of the three designs here for me this middle one gives us the best kind of compromise gives us a nice sense of three-dimensionality different shades of the same kind of color as opposed to going for either a pure white or a pure black the same thing goes when it comes to your text what we currently have on here on the right hand side on the black background is we're using pure white however when we've gone over to the one in the middle which is the better design that's not using pure white it's using a much lighter shade of a gray color so you can see if we take a look over on the right hand side we're using a very light shade of grey to reduce that potential eye strain based upon having strong contrast.

**Для Напарника:** Текст и поверхности держать в мягких оттенках, как в уже принятой логике коридора светлоты.



# 🎬 Design systems for infinity | Schema by Figma 2025 — Figma


### В чат-интерфейсе фильтры-чипы всё ещё лучше слов  ·  [15:25](https://youtu.be/eVmo7ncI08I?t=925)
**Мысль:** Когда варианты сложны и человек сам не знает, чего хочет, разговор не лучший способ выбора; старые метафоры (чипы) подходят.

> How would we display flight results in this experience? And we started talking with the team around sort of these little dynamic filter chips in flight results. It's a really old school metaphor to sort content. And at first we were sort of like why would we put chips filter chips on this really new conversant UI? Filtering should be conversational, right? But flight options are really complicated and sometimes you don't exactly know what you want and conversation is not always the best interaction material to use. Sometimes old school filters, old school metaphors with dynamic content are really the best. Remember, it's really about just matching a metaphor to a human need.

**Для Напарника:** Чат с напарником: выбор длительности и шага давать чипами в ленте, а не заставлять печатать.



# 🎬 Designing for Neurodiversity - Treyce M (Config 2022) — Figma


### Изобилие путей: разные представления для разных мозгов (Asana)  ·  [07:05](https://youtu.be/3aH--S3r9n4?t=425)
**Мысль:** Доска, список, календарь, таймлайн — не избыточность, а способы визуализировать работу под свой тип мышления.

> Is that your experience should be abundant right and so i like to my little sub head here is additional workflows increase usability right so the more the merrier here the first example that i have is from asana if you're not familiar saunas task management project management software and they have really built into their core product multiple views of ways to manage your work your tasks your goals whatever you're putting in there right part of their core experience is that you can see those things as a board as a list as a timeline on a calendar right some people might think that all of these different views are maybe a little redundant but i really like to think of them as abundant so the whatever type of person you are whatever way you're thinking whatever makes the most sense for you and your team you can visualize your work that way with asana which i think is so impressive and i'm so excited to see all the new views that they'll develop in the future.

**Для Напарника:** Дом/Мир могут давать разные способы увидеть прогресс (остров vs список шагов), но без перегруза выбором.


### Две кнопки закрытия в модалке Pinterest: избыточность помогает  ·  [08:10](https://youtu.be/3aH--S3r9n4?t=490)
**Мысль:** Дублирование выхода в двух привычных местах снимает когнитивный поиск; дизайнеры зря считают это излишеством.

> I wanted to like really zoom in on a very small way that you can bring abundance to your interface right so this is a modal from pinterest when you're creating a new board and the thing i absolutely love about this modal is it has a close button in the bottom left and in the top right now these buttons essentially do the same thing right there's a cancel button it'll stop this workflow close the modal the x at the top will also do the same thing this is probably my favorite example in my talk today because it's so simple but yet it actually will help anyone who's using this modal who needs to get out of it they don't have to think oh where's the close button right it's in two spots that these buttons are commonly placed so people will easily be able to find them and achieve their goal i can't tell you how many times i've had this discussion with fellow designers where they think again this might this these two buttons are a little redundant but i think that a certain amount of redundancy can really just help people right i don't think any user will look at this modal notice both close buttons and be offended or disappointed right i think it will only help them achieve their goals faster and with less frustration.

**Для Напарника:** Любой модальный экран Напарника — выход в привычных местах; «Не сегодня» всегда рядом и без вины.



# 🎬 Does Claude actually think like a designer? — Silu


### Награда не проверена исследованием; экран награды не должен быть дашбордом  ·  [06:20](https://youtu.be/_5EMOZGY9yg?t=380)
**Мысль:** «Посади дерево» — не гарантия мотивации, нужен ресёрч на целевых людях. На пике завершения три метрики сразу перегружают: момент должен быть лёгким праздником, а не отчётом.

> Coming back to this, what I question is, is it exciting enough to make someone want to come back every day? Does planting a tree motivate someone who's trying to finish a thesis chapter at midnight? and I don't think we can answer that without a without a user research. this needs a stronger, more validated answer, that Claude hasn't dive deep into. So, pushback number two is also around the rewards page. So, over here, it's showing me three or even four things at a once if we count the character and this whole celebration as well. It's showing me the points earned, the time it took, the time I invested, and a metaphorical weight I lifted off of my shoulders for completing these, task and leveling up. So, all three stat at once, the points, time, and that weight lifting off, all at that emotional peak of completing a task, I feel like that moment should feel lighter and more celebration and not like a dashboard. So, over here, I feel like there's a lot to look at. And, a lot of numbers to look at, basically. And in the prompts, I ask that, it should feel like there is like zero cognitive overload and I feel like this screen is working against that.

**Для Напарника:** RevealIsland: на финале один образ (остров растёт) без таблицы цифр; проверять награды на живых людях с СДВГ.


### Длинный список: нужен ответ на масштаб, видимый вес перетаскиваемого элемента, плавающая «добавить»  ·  [11:33](https://youtu.be/_5EMOZGY9yg?t=693)
**Мысль:** 15–20 пунктов теряют место и повышают нагрузку; нужны сворачивание или лимит ИИ. У drag-элемента должен быть визуальный вес, а кнопка «добавить» — закреплена в досягаемости.

> The first thing here is the problem with the long list. Right now, this works fine if there's only like five to seven levels, but what happens if there's like 15 to 20 levels? Users are scrolling up and down, they're losing their place, trying to remember how they reorder or edited. And that's adding a lot of cognitive load, which is what we're trying to eliminate here. So, the question I'm asking is should the list be collapsible into sections? Maybe in an accordion style. So, that users see only what's relevant at a given moment. Or there is a smarter solution like capping the number of levels that AI generates and asking the user if they want to go deeper. So, both have actually valid design directions, but right now this screen has no answer for scale and that's creating a gap. Second is the drag state to reorder the list. And it has added this overlay, which I really like. It really gives the element that you're trying to reorder a visual weight that makes it clear of what's in motion and it differentiates it from other static elements. And that's very good use of visual feedback to support the interaction model and I would keep this design. And the third one. We have this button here. I know we can't see it, but I'm sure this is the add new level button that is just hidden behind this main one. and right now, this is sitting at the bottom of the list, which works well if there's always just five to seven items and this add level button is always going to be visible in the screen no matter which size of screen it is. But on a long list, the user would actually have to scroll all the way to the end every time they want to add something new. And that's a discoverability problem. And it breaks the flow. A better solution here is adding a floating action button that stays anchored to the screen regardless of the scroll position and it's always reachable, ergonomic, and it doesn't compete with the list content. That's a standard pattern for a reason. It solves exactly this problem.

**Для Напарника:** Список микрошагов: ограничить число шагов от ИИ (3–5, «глубже?» по запросу), перетаскиваемый шаг приподнимать тенью, «добавить шаг» — якорная кнопка.



# 🎬 Every UI/UX Concept Explained in Under 10 Minutes — Kole Jain


### Сигнифаеры: интерфейс сам объясняет, как им пользоваться  ·  [00:00](https://youtu.be/EcbgbKtOELY?t=0)
**Мысль:** Контейнер, выделение активного и серый неактивный текст объясняют работу без инструкции. Состояния нажатия, подсветки и подсказки сообщают, что элемент умеет.

> This one's easiest to explain with an example first. If I have three text and icon pairs, but I put a container around drinks and food, you know that they're related and dessert is not. Then, if I put a container around drinks, you know that it's selected and you can toggle over to food. If I gray this text out, you know that it's inactive and clicking it probably won't do anything. See how you just knew all of that? I didn't need to write instructions on how it worked because the UI was signifying how things worked. Good UI has many signifiers, like a button press state, highlights on active nav items, hover states, or even tooltips to tell the user what a UI affords or what it can do.

**Для Напарника:** Чипы длительности и кнопки на Фокусе должны сами показывать выбранное и неактивное состояние, без пояснительных надписей.


### Иерархия создаётся контрастом размера, позиции и цвета  ·  [00:47](https://youtu.be/EcbgbKtOELY?t=47)
**Мысль:** Самое важное крупно и вверху, второстепенное мельче и ниже, а акцентный элемент отличается цветом. Именно разница между большим и малым создаёт иерархию.

> Now, take a look at this card. It displays all of the information in a logical, repeatable format, but it looks like a spreadsheet, not a design. So, let's give it some hierarchy, which is pretty intuitive. We have size, position, and color to use to our advantage. Starting from the top, an image always adds a great pop of color and makes scanning super easy, just like Uber. If in this case the item is the most important, we want that large and bold and at the top because otherwise it'll blend in with something like the time and date, which can be below and much smaller. And it's this contrast, the difference between small and big or colorful and not, that actually creates the hierarchy. It's also why price should be at the top, right aligned, and blue. It's different than the rest, which draws the eye to it. The location should be smaller and below like this.

**Для Напарника:** В reward-карточке и карточках шагов выделять одну главную строку размером и позицией, остальное делать меньше и тише.


### Сетка лишь ориентир; важнее белое пространство и группировка  ·  [02:05](https://youtu.be/EcbgbKtOELY?t=125)
**Мысль:** Не всё должно ложиться в 12 колонок, а кратность 4 нужна, чтобы значения делились пополам и давали согласованность. Группировка близких элементов тоже форма иерархии.

> Now, some people have this misconception that all of your content needs to align to a 12-column grid and be exactly eight pixels apart. But, these are just guidelines. For example, this design doesn't line up with any of the columns and that's not uncommon, especially for custom landing pages. But, for highly structured pages like galleries, blogs, or any repeating content, they can actually be useful for responsiveness by giving guidelines for responsive behavior on tablet with eight columns and mobile with four. But, much more important than using excessive grids and layouts is white space, letting things breathe. For example, in a simple section like this, the large text would have a font size and line height like this and the subtext would have a font size and line height like so. Buttons would be similar and optionally a top announcement bar like this. Then, simply 32 pixels between every item, but you can also group elements that just go together like the announcement and text or the text and subtext, which is just another form of visual hierarchy. And this is where we see the four-point grid system actually come into play. Everything is a multiple, not because it inherently looks better, but because you can always split things in half,.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать шаг 4/8 пикселей и группировать связанные элементы, а между блоками оставлять воздух, особенно на Доме.


### Не больше шести размеров шрифта; в плотных экранах диапазон меньше  ·  [04:06](https://youtu.be/EcbgbKtOELY?t=246)
**Мысль:** Для лендингов диапазон размеров большой, для дашбордов он сжимается (обычно до 24px) из-за плотности информации.

> Text sizes can vary wildly. For landing pages and websites, you generally don't want more than six font sizes, which can have a really large range. For things like dashboards, that range shrinks dramatically to where you don't normally have text sizes larger than 24 pixels because of the increase in information density.

**Для Напарника:** Ограничить шкалу типографики Напарника шестью размерами и не раздувать размеры на плотных экранах вроде Мира.


### Тёмная тема: глубину даёт более светлая карточка, а не тень  ·  [05:44](https://youtu.be/EcbgbKtOELY?t=344)
**Мысль:** В тёмном режиме тени не работают, поэтому карточка светлее фона, границы слабее, а чипы приглушены по насыщенности и яркости. Фон может быть не только серым, но и глубоким фиолетовым или зелёным.

> Now, designing in dark mode presents some pretty interesting challenges for us. First, this card is using a light border, which creates a bit too much contrast. So, we'll lower that down. In dark mode, we don't have shadows like light mode, so to create depth, we need to have a lighter card than the background. So, I'll make that adjustment. This chip is also far too bright, so we can dim down the saturation and brightness and flip that for the text to create some hierarchy. There's also a ton of flexibility for deep purples, reds, or greens,.

**Для Напарника:** Для карточек над AppBackdrop поднимать светлоту карточки на малый шаг и приглушать границы, не меняя коридор фона.


### Тени: меньше непрозрачности, больше блюра; тень не должна бросаться в глаза  ·  [06:16](https://youtu.be/EcbgbKtOELY?t=376)
**Мысль:** Карточкам нужна слабая тень, а попапам сильнее. Если тень видна первой, она использована неверно.

> But, speaking of depth, shadows are a fantastic tool to use on light mode. This shadow, along with most, is too strong. So, reducing the opacity and dialing up the blur helps a lot. The strength you'll need depends on the foreground and background, but cards require less while content that sits above other content like popovers will need stronger shadows. We can even use these techniques with inner and outer shadows to create effects like raised tactile buttons. Just remember, if the shadow is the first thing you notice on a design, you're not using it right.

**Для Напарника:** Для всплывающих элементов использовать мягкие широкие тени низкой непрозрачности, чтобы они не конфликтовали с живым фоном.



# 🎬 Everything you need to Design macOS Apps EXACTLY like Apple (beginner friendly) — Kole Jain


### Пустое состояние и поэтапное раскрытие: показывай только нужное сейчас  ·  [03:27](https://youtu.be/Vy0KKvZJRH8?t=207)
**Мысль:** Пока нет данных, фильтры и компоненты скрыты; данные показываются по клику. Вопрос: какой минимум интерфейса позволяет контенту сиять.

> Just like when you get a clear empty state when you have no notes, we'll have a simple empty state inviting the user to start saving inspiration. This is a form of progressive disclosure, only showing the user what they need to see in that moment. All of the filters and other components are hidden because they simply aren't useful until the user starts saving inspiration. Another good example of this is when we do have images only showing all of the data when the user actually clicks on the image. So before adding colors or effects, ask yourself this. What is the minimum UI needed to really let that content shine?

**Для Напарника:** Пустой остров и пустой план: «Плана нет, и это ноль», приглашение начать; остальное раскрывается по мере появления данных.



# 🎬 Everything you need to know about Mobile App UI’s in 8 minutes (beginner friendly) — Kole Jain


### Нижняя панель: 3–5 пунктов, цель касания больше 44px  ·  [00:23](https://youtu.be/Gfsd8NNuD9g?t=23)
**Мысль:** На мобильном вместо сайдбара плавающий нижний бар, где пять ссылок это предел, а три-четыре идеал; это гарантирует цель больше 44 пикселей.

> Obviously the navigation. Where we had an expansive sidebar, we now definitely do not. So, we have two options. If we can consolidate our sidebar links down to just a few key icons, we can go with a nice bottom bar, which nowadays is typically floating with the important action broken out just like this. Five links down here is pretty much the definite limit, and three or four is much more ideal. But, either way, this means that we'll always maintain over a 44-pixel target since people do have fat fingers.

**Для Напарника:** AppNav с тремя пунктами (Дом, Фокус, Мир) уже в идеале; проверить высоту цели касания не менее 44px.


### На телефоне крупнее шрифт и выбор одного: один блок, одно направление  ·  [01:37](https://youtu.be/Gfsd8NNuD9g?t=97)
**Мысль:** База шрифта iOS 17px против 13px у macOS, поэтому на малых экранах всё крупнее. Из набора десктопных блоков остаётся один, и секция течёт либо вертикально, либо горизонтально, но не в обе стороны.

> Despite our screen getting much, much smaller, the type scale and spacing remains relatively similar to desktop, even getting a bit larger. iOS actually has a base font size of 17 pixels, while macOS has a base of only 13 pixels. So, more often than not, things actually get larger on these smaller screens. And it's a tough realization that on desktop, where you could have had an action bar, a gallery of recent notes, a full calendar, tasks, and a scratch pad, now you can pick one of those things. And because we have to pick just one, the way we start to lay out content starts to change a little. Dashboards and desktop designs can lay out content in two directions at the same time. What that means is this layout has two columns and three rows, obviously extending in both directions. However, on mobile, you can choose one direction to move in per section. Either you can stack each note vertically, or you can make it horizontal off the page, but of course, you can't have both like you reasonably could on desktop. This is a useful guideline when taking content from dashboards and making it mobile-optimized. And if you understand that concept, things really start to become a piece of cake.

**Для Напарника:** Не втискивать в Дом много блоков: на 390px показывать одну главную цель и один способ прокрутки в секции.


### Карточки как основной блок; не вкладывай карточки друг в друга  ·  [02:46](https://youtu.be/Gfsd8NNuD9g?t=166)
**Мысль:** Карточки группируют содержимое вместо пустого пространства, но двойная вложенность создаёт отступ на отступе и сжимает место. Часто группировку можно сделать пробелом.

> There's really only four types of building blocks for an app. The biggest and most important are cards. Basically, everything on here is a card in some way or another because it allows us to flexibly group content in place of white space, which we don't typically have a lot of here. The others are text or links, of course, images, and inputs. And of course, any of those can be put into cards as well. One important note about cards is to try and avoid double-nesting them, kind of like this. It's not always avoidable, but it creates padding on padding, which really restricts the space you have and ends up cramping things. Often, you can group with white space instead of a container and somewhat alleviate this problem.

**Для Напарника:** Не класть карточки в карточки на Доме и Фокусе; группировать связанные кнопки расстоянием.


### Один экран делает одно; контекстный выбор через bottom sheet  ·  [03:29](https://youtu.be/Gfsd8NNuD9g?t=209)
**Мысль:** Настройки это только настройки, редактор это только редактор. Когда нужен выбор в контексте, нижняя шторка оставляет пользователя на месте.

> One screen does one thing, and that's it. Obviously, the very notable exception is our home screen, but otherwise, the settings is just settings, and the notes editor is just a notes editor. We don't throw in clutter like recent notes or suggested templates at the top. Instead, whenever you need to add something new, don't reach for a different layout, reach for a different page altogether. Though, this example about templates brings up an interesting point about context. We're editing a note, so we don't want to be ripped away to a new page to select a template, but we don't have enough room on screen. This is where bottom sheets become super useful. This is a bottom sheet. You've probably seen it a bunch, and now we can simply add in a title, search bar, and a check and X, and all of our templates, too. These can be any height, but they keep the user in context and are easy to navigate with.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус остаётся экраном одной цели; выбор длительности и шагов можно вынести в нижнюю шторку, а не на отдельную страницу.



# 🎬 Fastest Way to Fix a Landing Page That's Not Converting — Ansub Khan | Supafast


### Hero из четырёх элементов по порядку: заголовок, для кого и что получит, доказательство, кнопка  ·  [01:19](https://youtu.be/Uko0mLSKrRY?t=79)
**Мысль:** Без явного «для кого и что получит» покупатель не скроллит; проверять текст без дизайна.

> If you strip away the design of the hero section, that leaves us with just three lines, a small label, a slogan, and a button. So, there's no line saying who's it for, what it does. It's not there, and buyers now have to scroll down to learn that. And most of them, they don't do that. A hero section that works have four things in order: a headline, who's it for and what they get, and proof, and the button. And if you see our redesign, it basically has all four of them right away. A clear heading, a clear indicator of what's happening. We're also telling them who's it for, and what they get. And now, we're also telling them who uses it, which is our proof. And a clear button which says what exactly is going to happen. So, write your hero section as a plain text before you design it. Don't worry about how it will look. Just see the text.

**Для Напарника:** Сначала написать текст героя лендинга без дизайна и проверить по этим четырём пунктам.



# 🎬 Fitts’s Law in Touchscreen UX (Why Mobile Design Rules Are Different) — Khushi Darbar


### Главное в центре экрана, вторичное по краям, редкое в углах  ·  [04:47](https://youtu.be/XcyVnia0F_4?t=287)
**Мысль:** Чаще и точнее всего нажимают в центр; основная кнопка и контент идут в центр, табы и поиск на края, редко нужное (настройки) в углы.

> This brings us to the biggest bombshell from all the research. When you look at where we actually tap most often and with the most accuracy, it isn't the bottom of the screen. It's the center, right in the middle. I mean, think about it. You instinctively scroll a link or a button to the middle of your screen before you tap it. That is the real prime real estate on your phone. Okay. So, if the thumb zone is dead, what are the new rules? What does all this mean for designing apps that actually feel good to use? apps that are designed for human hands and not for a little computer mouse. The new framework is actually beautifully simple and it completely flips the old model upside down. Your most important stuff, the main content, the primary buttons, that goes right in the center where we're already looking and where we tap the most accurately. Secondary things like tab bars or search, they go on the edges. And the corners, which are actually the hardest places for us to tap, are saved for things you rarely need, like a settings menu. This table just sums up the whole mindbending shift perfectly. With a mouse, the edges were magic. With our fingers, the center is king and the edges are awkward.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус: главная кнопка (Старт, Готово) ближе к центру; AppNav остаётся вторичным краем, настройки не выносить на видное место.

### Границы интерактивного элемента нужны, иначе кнопка выглядит текстом  ·  [04:04](https://youtu.be/gdohlUn7fVQ?t=244)
**Мысль:** Помимо текста, сам контрол должен контрастировать с фоном минимум 3:1, иначе пользователь не отличит кнопку от обычного текста.

> Whenever there's interactive control, you're gonna need to clearly show the boundary of that control. Otherwise people won't see the background of the button. They just see the text. That way they might not know if that's a button or just plain text.

**Для Напарника:** Чипы длительности и вторичные кнопки на тёмном фоне: тонкая обводка или заливка с контрастом ≥3:1.

### Не веди новичка в тупик, особенно в первой сессии  ·  [06:37](https://youtu.be/XtpCUmsVMcc?t=397)
**Мысль:** Крупный заметный элемент, ведущий на заблокированное, тратит тапы и сбивает с толку; главный сигнал должен вести к реальной ценности.

> Me to tap on something where everything is locked. Basically, here, if you see the screen, there's two things that they want you to tap, or at least it's this is what they're signaling. The big orange box and the circle star. But, the big orange box actually does not do anything cuz everything is locked inside of it. So, this is just a big waste of empty space and of CTA when the main thing you're calling for attention is actually a dead end. Never lead users to dead ends, especially when it's their first session, cuz they're going to feel confused and they're going to waste taps. You do not users feeling confused or wasting taps, so never lead them to dead end.

**Для Напарника:** Самая заметная кнопка на Доме должна запускать Фокус, и нигде нет заметных, но недоступных элементов.
