**Мысль:** Спроси, что самое важное в экране; если это текст задания, его нельзя усекать многоточием.

> Okay, so this here's a good example where the design could be better just with a little bit more thought put into it cuz if you're designing something to tell a user what a quest is, then the first question you should ask is, "Okay, what is the most important part of the design?" And the most important part is telling the user what the quest is. Therefore, you cannot truncate it. Like you cannot truncate the best part of the design, the most important part.

**Для Напарника:** Текст шага/задачи на экране Фокуса показывать целиком (перенос строк), а не усекать.



# 🎬 Graphic Design Principles Master-Class! (Ep 1 White Space) — Satori Graphics


### Группировка близостью и проверка прищуриванием  ·  [01:26](https://youtu.be/FDFD2-poyGw?t=86)
**Мысль:** Близкие элементы воспринимаются как группа. Если прищуриться, структура должна читаться по одному размещению пробелов, ещё до чтения текста.

> In this stage, we use whites space to create groups or sections. This is where things like the Gestto principles come into play, especially proximity. Elements that are close together are seen as a group. Elements that are spaced out are seen as separate. So on your layouts, you might try grouping a heading with a subheading and then giving a bit of space for the body text below. Think of it like a table seating area. People sitting close are probably talking to each other. And when you apply this visually, it becomes a conversation between design elements, and the viewer instantly understands how your content is structured. To sharpen this even more, try this. squint your eyes and look at your layout in a blurry way. You should still be able to tell which elements belong together just by how the white space shapes the page. That's the power of grouping or proximity. It should work even before you read a single word.

**Для Напарника:** Каждый экран проверять блюром или прищуриванием: видны ли группы без чтения.


### Пространство направляет внимание без стрелок и цвета  ·  [02:47](https://youtu.be/FDFD2-poyGw?t=167)
**Мысль:** Дать кнопке больше воздуха значит привлечь к ней взгляд; уменьшить зазор значит связать заголовок с абзацем. Слабая иерархия заставляет пользователя работать.

> It can direct focus without using arrows, colors, or extra graphics. Let's say you have a heading, a paragraph, and a button on a UI. By adjusting the spacing between them, you can decide what gets noticed first. And if you want to highlight that button, you might want to give it some generous breathing room. And if you want to tie the headings closely to the paragraph, just reduce that gap. A design with poor hierarchy forces the user to work extra effort on your design. And a design with smart white space guides them naturally. You're leading the eye like a dance partner just by using spacing.

**Для Напарника:** Главную кнопку старта окружить заметно большим воздухом, чем остальные элементы Фокуса.



# 🎬 Hierarchy (Typographic Focus) — Lewis Franklin


### Иерархия задаёт порядок чтения и позволяет сканировать (заголовок, подзаголовки, текст)  ·  [00:28](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=28)
**Мысль:** Иерархия говорит, куда смотреть и что важно; дизайнер может предсказать, что прочтут первым; заголовок - самый большой, по нему решают, читать ли дальше, подзаголовки дают ключевые мысли.

> So a visual hierarchy is an important element in any design project it's going to tell where to look and what things on the screen of printed page are most important hierarchy gives readers a sense of how to actually read the material from start to finish with visual cues and flow type hierarchy organizes and gives order to the text elements in your design just as web designers and web developers use something called header tags which are like h1 h2 and h3 and so on to organize the importance of the text visual hierarchy uses visual cues in addition type hierarchy helps reader scan text reading bits of type faster in chunks that look alike generally english-language readers start at the top left and read across and down type is often organized to mirror this behavior but what if the biggest and boldest text is midway down the page well then a reader will start there and go back to the top of the page and continue the normal reading behavior hierarchy is important because it allows the designer to determine what someone will likely read first what they will read second and so on because of this the designer can create type in such a way that he or she knows what information is likely to be received and in what order as creating hierarchy is important to designers it is equally important to readers good visual hierarchy tells us what is important making reading much easier for example you know that the headline is the most important part of a story it's its attention-grabbing because it is the biggest followed by sub heads then body text for scalability purpose you could read the big text to get an idea if the article would interest you before investing a bunch of time and the copy and then the sub heads lets you figure out you know some key points within that text and then if you decide at that point to read it then you can read the text.

**Для Напарника:** Три уровня считывания на каждом экране Напарника: сначала главное (что делать сейчас), потом детали.


### Иерархия из размера, веса, цвета, текстуры, начертания, ориентации и пространства; размер должен заметно различаться  ·  [03:10](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=190)
**Мысль:** Читатели видят самое крупное первым, но разница размеров должна быть значимой, а разные шрифты масштабируются по-разному.

> No one-step solution to creating visual hierarchy with text it can be created with size weight color texture typeface choices and combinations orientation and space were a combination of all of that we will start with size one of the simplest ways to establish hierarchy with type is through size readers will often see the biggest type first and start reading from there in relation to size scale is equally important to create distinctive hierarchy sizes must have significant variance in relationship to other type on the page the thing to keep in mind is that different typefaces may scale differently so just changing the point size may not be enough you need to look at how typefaces work together to ensure the size and scale are properly balanced.

**Для Напарника:** Контролировать, чтобы ступени размеров заголовок/текст были заметными, а не 2 px разницы.


### Размеры от основного текста в процентах: заголовок +250%, подзаголовок +175%, меню +140%  ·  [04:19](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=259)
**Мысль:** Строить шкалу от базового текста в процентах, а не в абсолютных значениях; (цифры для навигации в расшифровке искажены).

> Well you start with the main body text and go from there and for the text elements that are used to really draw people in go big or go home here are a few percentages that can help you get started with main body being somewhere around you know 11 points the main headers are supposed to be about two hundred and fifty percent bigger secondary headers 175 percent bigger navigation elements six hundred and sixty-five percent bigger secondary navigation or menus is a hundred and forty percent bigger so of course those are percentages because we're not basing it on a particular font size because we know that body copy is supposed to be around 11 or 10 points.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать как ориентир для множителей шкалы заголовков относительно базового размера.


### Пары шрифтов: контраст по весу при одном тоне и x-height; толщина создаёт иерархию  ·  [05:31](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=331)
**Мысль:** Гармоничная пара - разные по весу, но близкие по характеру и высоте строчных; жирный выглядит важнее и крупнее, тонкий - меньше.

> Typefaces are the most used element in terms of creating typographic hierarchy the key is to have contrasts between typefaces this is one reason that designers often select one serif and one sans-serif typeface for a project for the best combinations look for typefaces that are different in weight but have a similar tone or feel if you were using rounded typefaces stick to letter forms with similar shapes in the O's for example opt for typefaces with similar X Heights if the style will be used in line with one or another throughout the entire project and we have talked about this in the past where like palatino and Helvetica or a good combination palutena are etiquette and Garamond are a good combination okay because the letters kind of match the x-height it has a good feel but it has stark contrast in what you're trying to get across wait the thickness of strokes in the type you select for a project can create hierarchy as well the thicker the typeface the more bold and bigger it appears light condensed and thin typefaces often appear smaller than they actually are bold ultra and hammerhead typefaces can appear larger than the point size may represent how large or small a typeface appears by weight is also relative to the other typefaces used in the project so pairing thick and thin typefaces immediately causes a sense of hierarchy with the thick style carrying the look of more important often even though it is actually smaller on the display.

**Для Напарника:** Подбирать второй шрифт или начертание с похожим x-height; вес использовать как инструмент вместо увеличения размера.


### Цвет в иерархии: тёплое выходит вперёд, холодное отступает; насыщенное поднимается над приглушённым  ·  [07:53](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=473)
**Мысль:** Тёплые цвета выдвигаются на передний план, холодные отходят назад; насыщенные яркие поднимаются над приглушёнными.

> Also add emphasis and increase weight to type think about some of the color rules you learned as a child warm colors the Reds the yellows the Browns will have more pop while cool colors like blues purples and grays will fade to the back warm to the front cold to the back when using color to establish hierarchy consider the hues used for the foreground type and background color contrast will also play an important role the more saturated or brightest colors will often lift off the screen against more muted tones.

**Для Напарника:** Акцент на главную кнопку Фокуса тёплым насыщенным цветом на холодно-приглушённом фоне.


### Пространство как инструмент иерархии: группируй близко, крупному - больше воздуха  ·  [09:39](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=579)
**Мысль:** Воздух делает текст крупнее и читаемее; похожие элементы держат ближе, чем чужие; жирному, большому нужно больше места.

> White space can make type appear bigger and more readable lack of white space can make it feel tighter and smaller use space to your advantage when creating a sense of hierarchy think about the space you use between lines of text think about kerning for the biggest type styles think about the spatial relationship between letters and the edge of the canvas think about the relationships between type of different sizes styles and colors every single space can impact your hierarchical scale keep similar-type grouped closer together and with less space than unrelated items and design your space or the text falls in a distinct order bigger bolder brighter text aalam elements often need more space than smaller simpler text blocks.

**Для Напарника:** Крупные элементы Дома (reward-карточка) окружить большим воздухом; близкие элементы - ближе друг к другу.


### Три уровня типографики: первичный (вход), вторичный (подписи), основной текст обязан читаться  ·  [11:52](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=712)
**Мысль:** Достаточно трёх уровней: крупный привлекает, вторичный - подзаголовки и навигация, тело текста главное - чтобы читалось.

> Most designs can be successful with three levels of typographic high ography ID hierarchy vanilla those levels are primary type secondary type tertiary type okay primary type is often the typography on the page with the most visual way such as main headers or display quotes the purpose of primary type is to bring readers into the overall design secondary type is everything else that is not the main content this can include captions sub heads and navigational or static type elements tertiary type is the main body copy there is one thing to remember about tertiary copy it must be readable little else matters when it comes to the design.

**Для Напарника:** Не плодить больше трёх уровней текста на экране Фокуса.


### Первичный крючок - вторичный - третичный: что видно за секунду, затем детали  ·  [14:29](https://youtu.be/xSRsdgx5s48?t=869)
**Мысль:** Композиция: что я вижу первым, следующим, и последнее, что возвращает к идее; сначала привлёк внимание, потом объясняешь.

> A composition has to have a primary draw a secondary concept and a tertiary concept what am I supposed to look at first what am I supposed to see next and what is the final thing I'm supposed to see it's supposed to hook me all the way back in to the original concept when we look at posters what makes us see the poster on the board then what draws us into once we're at the toaster what's the one thing that we can see in eight seconds or not even eight seconds just in an instant okay that's the next most important thing exactly when and where it is those are tertiary concepts you got our attention you gave us something to do now tell us a little bit more about it.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг: за секунду - кот и остров с одной фразой, дальше объяснение механики.



# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal | Full Audiobook 🎧 — WE WE TV


### Перегруженная главная Twitter 2009 против простейшего действия  ·  [03:07](https://youtu.be/TW_yZhdWt0g?t=187)
**Мысль:** Мотивация дорога и тяжело меняется, а лёгкость действия решает; перегруженный экран заставляет читать и осмыслять до действия.

> Once a user is prompted, they have to actually do something. And this action has to be the absolute simplest behavior done in anticipation of reward. Twitter's 2009 homepage is actually a brilliant example of what not to do here. If you remember it, it was highly cluttered. There's text everywhere, links, an embedded video, a search bar. It's just overwhelming. For a brand new user, this design demanded way too much cognitive effort. It forced them to stop, read, and comprehend before they could do anything, which severely limited their ability to just take action. Twitter quickly learned they had to simplify this drastically. Because here's the formula. For any action to occur, the user needs sufficient motivation and the ability to do it. But motivation, motivation is expensive and really hard to change. Ability on the other hand, making things ridiculously easy to do, like clicking a single button or endlessly scrolling a feed, that is where habit-forming products excel. Make it simple and the action happens. Basically, reducing the effort required to perform an action is infinitely more effective than trying to hype someone up to do it,.

**Для Напарника:** Дом: одно простое действие сверху; не показывать десяток карточек новому человеку.



# 🎬 How a Niche Aviation App Became a Masterclass in App UI Design | App Breakdown #84 — App Breakdown


### Жирный шрифт везде красив, но иерархия слабеет  ·  [09:08](https://youtu.be/VXAR-ueRW1U?t=548)
**Мысль:** Если всё выделено, ничего не выделено: чрезмерный акцент размывает иерархию, хотя чистота делает это приемлемым.

> I love just how bold they went with the typeface everywhere. Maybe it's a bit too much, like they give a bit of too much importance to everything, and so things kind of lose the hierarchy, but it's so clean that I really love it.

**Для Напарника:** В Доме и Фокусе ограничить число жирных/крупных элементов до одного главного на экран; остальное спокойнее, чтобы ключевое действие читалось.


### Плотность данных растёт при скролле: сверху главное и просторно, ниже плотные строки  ·  [11:35](https://youtu.be/VXAR-ueRW1U?t=695)
**Мысль:** Посещённые страницы становятся приглушённо-серыми, чтобы было видно, где был; на длинной странице верх с малой плотностью для важного, дальше плотность растёт.

> I love this loading of the timeline at the bottom. And notice how the pages that I've already visited turn into this more muted gray. It's a nice touch for you to know where you've been. And if we keep scrolling, it seems like the data density increases. And so I'm assuming we're going to get to a point where it's just rows. So if we keep scrolling, now we have planes. Yep, and now we have just rows. So it's a good rule of thumb that usually whenever you have a big scroll view, you start with less data density up top so the things that really matter and that you want to give more space for and then data density increases as the user scrolls on the page.

**Для Напарника:** Лента реплик в чате: свежее и важное крупно, старые реплики тускнеют (depth-fade уже есть). Подтверждает направление ChatBubble.


### Единая оболочка (sheet) для всего контента: быстро осваивается  ·  [13:04](https://youtu.be/VXAR-ueRW1U?t=784)
**Мысль:** Когда любое содержимое открывается в одной и той же форме, пользователь всегда знает, где он, и быстро осваивает интерфейс.

> So it seems like the entirety of the app is a shell into these sheets, which is cool because you always know where you are and you become familiar with the interface very fast. I've been recording this just for a few minutes and I feel like I can navigate the app very easily.

**Для Напарника:** Три экрана /app, /session, /world делят AppBackdrop и AppNav; держать единообразные паттерны открытия деталей, чтобы не плодить новые формы.



# 🎬 How Do You Design Accessible Filters for Cognitive Disabilities? - Web Accessibility For All — Ariel Ferro


### Сброс должен быть заметен для людей с проблемами рабочей памяти  ·  [06:24](https://youtu.be/9E6jKXq97mY?t=384)
**Мысль:** Слабо заметная кнопка «Очистить» — кошмар для тех, кто не помнит, что выбрал и как начать заново.

> Pitfall 3, unclear reset functionality: How often do you see a Clear Filters button that is barely visible? For users with executive function difficulties, or short-term memory challenges, this is a nightmare! They can't remember what they chose and can't figure out how to start over. Always offer a Reset or Clear All function. Make it visible. Announce it. Clearly.

**Для Напарника:** На экране Фокуса дать понятный «начать заново/сменить шаг» без наказания.


### Показывать активные настройки простым текстом и давать откат  ·  [07:35](https://youtu.be/9E6jKXq97mY?t=455)
**Мысль:** Невидимые состояния по умолчанию превращают фильтр в судоку; активные значения нужно показывать явно и легко отменять.

> Problem one filtering is often a puzzle not a tool filtering interfaces can feel like sudoku especially for users with cognitive or executive function disabilities too many choices filters that interact in weird ways or unclear default states turn what should be a helpful refinement tool into a wall of options design tip make defaults visible show active filters in plain text and always give users an easy way to backtrack.

**Для Напарника:** Выбранную длительность/режим на экране Фокуса показывать текстом, с лёгким возвратом назад.


### Доступность — это агентность, а не только метки  ·  [12:18](https://youtu.be/9E6jKXq97mY?t=738)
**Мысль:** Людям нужно пространство подумать, чёткие контрольные точки и возможность восстановиться; ясность важнее минимализма.

> Wrapping up: Accessibility isn't just about labeling things right. It's about agency. Can users do what they need to do in the way that makes the most sense for them? Can they recover from mistakes without starting over? Can they move at their own pace using their preferred input method? Filtering, sorting and search often fail not because they are hard to use, but because they are rigid! We want to reduce clicks, But people needs space to think. We want instant results, but some users need clear checkpoints. We want minimal UIs, but clarity and affordance matter more than clean lines.

**Для Напарника:** Не прятать важные действия ради «чистоты» интерфейса; ясность и понятность кнопок — приоритет.



# 🎬 How I Fixed a Client's Onboarding and Won the Contract — Bear Liu


### Не просить запоминать и не объяснять интерфейс интерфейсом  ·  [08:33](https://youtu.be/S7eGDOkd_Pg?t=513)
**Мысль:** Ментальная энергия ограничена: чем проще, тем лучше. Если интерфейс настолько сложен, что для объяснения нужен ещё слой (тултипы, карусели), надо упростить первый слой, а не добавлять второй.

> And another typical onboarding pitfall we have, besides being all about features, is also something around asking people to memorize something, and this is also not recommended because — I don't say people are lazy, just people have limited mental load, right, so we have very short attention spans and we have very limited time and effort to remember or to think about something. We want to save that energy, save the energy for the very important things. So the simpler, the easier it is for people, the better the results will be, right. And also the worst thing about the onboarding experience is to use an interface to explain another interface. This happened to me a while ago when I just started doing UI design and I thought it was really cool — it seemed like every software app used tooltips, they used something like this to introduce, oh, this is something you can do, and this is a carousel people can swipe to see different things. But this is definitely not what we want to do. If your interface is so complicated that you need to use another interface to explain it, you probably want to improve the first layer interface and not add another layer.

**Для Напарника:** Внутренние экраны Напарника должны быть понятны без подсказок: одна цель на экран, короткие подписи. Для СДВГ-аудитории нагрузка на рабочую память особенно критична.



# 🎬 How the Braun TS 45 Reflects Rams’ 10 Principles of Good Design — A Wandanlage Case Study — Novalux Stereophonic


### Форма элемента подсказывает действие: кнопка нажимается, ручка крутится  ·  [05:00](https://youtu.be/vYOUPm8S7mo?t=300)
**Мысль:** Выключатель отделён цветом и положением, форма колпачка показывает «нажми» или «крути», индикатор показывает ограниченный диапазон; пользователь начинает пользоваться без вопросов.

> Before I got into repairing audio for a living, I worked in the home automation industry. I programmed lighting, shade, and AV control systems, and a big portion of my job was training customers how to use their brand new system at the end of a project. Whether it be a handheld remote control, a touchscreen, a tablet, I always tried to design simple user interfaces that the customer could just pick up and use. My goal was to be able to hand them that user interface and have them start interacting and using their system without asking too many questions. And that's exactly what this interface pulls off. So, let's look at this in a little bit more detail. Let's start out with the power switch. Notice how it's separated from the rest of the controls, and it's a different color. It's function is kind of self-evident, and we even know what to do with it because it has a scooped front section, so you know this is going to be a switch that we push. Same thing goes for these selections up here. We know these are push buttons because of the way that the switch cap is shaped. All these other ones are round, so we know we're going to spin those. And then the selector switch has basically an indicator on it. Which tells us that it's not going to rotate 360. It's only going to rotate within this range. So, really well done on the control design.

**Для Напарника:** Кнопки и пикер длительности в Фокусе должны сами сообщать, что с ними делать (форма, иконка, положение), без пояснительного текста.


### Самые частые варианты рядом, лишние режимы убраны  ·  [06:27](https://youtu.be/vYOUPm8S7mo?t=387)
**Мысль:** Режим моно встроили в переключатель источника, а два основных источника поставили рядом, чтобы реже двигать переключатель.

> Instead of having a separate mode switch, the mono summing function is built into the switch itself. So, they have six selections here, the mono and stereo version of each source, but it's not staggered, mono stereo, mono stereo, mono stereo. They put the stereo tuner and the stereo turntable input right next to one another, and those are going to be the most common inputs. So, this I'm sure was thought about so that you don't have to move the switch all that much. Your two primary sources are right next to each other, and I think that was a really great thought.

**Для Напарника:** В пикере длительности держать самый частый выбор (25 мин, рекомендуем) рядом с точкой входа и не добавлять отдельный режим там, где можно встроить в основной контрол.


### Группировать по частоте: ежедневное рядом, «настроил и забыл» отдельно  ·  [07:21](https://youtu.be/vYOUPm8S7mo?t=441)
**Мысль:** Громкость и выбор источника стоят рядом, потому что их трогают каждый день; тембр и баланс настраиваются один раз. Логический порядок позволяет сразу начать пользоваться.

> The remaining controls are volume, balance, treble, and bass. And these controls all affect the overall sound of the system, so they're all located together. These three are set it and forget it controls that are based on personal preference in your specific system. So, balance for example, this might be tweaked one way or the other to compensate for a listening position that wasn't quite centered. And one interesting thing to note about this control, it doesn't work like a typical balance control. If I come all the way to here, it's not going to completely mute out the right speaker. It's just a subtle change one way or the other. Notice how the volume control is right next to the selector switch. So, in your normal day-to-day use of this device, you're going to power it on and then hang out around these two controls and that's basically all you need to do. If you want to change station, you go over here, but once you're set up for your room and your preference, you really don't need to touch these controls. So, very simple design, very easy to understand, everything's put in a logical order so that people can just walk up to this and start using it.

**Для Напарника:** Ежедневные действия на Доме (написать, начать Фокус) держать у большого пальца; редкие настройки (имя, чек-ин) убирать в отдельное место.



# 🎬 How To 4X Your Shopify Conversion Rate (Complete CRO Guide) — Davie Fogarty Mentors


### Hero: заголовок, подзаголовок «почему это важно», продукт в центре, одно действие  ·  [14:18](https://youtu.be/A-7pTamjBIw?t=858)
**Мысль:** Первый экран — шанс (иногда единственный) ответить на главные вопросы: что это, зачем мне, что делать.

> The hero section of your homepage is your first and sometimes only charts to answer those questions. Start with a clear headline, tell you exactly what you offer. Add a sub headline that explains why it matters to them. And then show your product front and center in the hero image. And give them one obvious action like shop now.

**Для Напарника:** Hero лендинга Напарника: заголовок, подзаголовок, кот с островом и одна кнопка «Начать фокус».


### Лёгкая страница: длинный текст прячется в аккордеон, раскрывается по желанию  ·  [15:17](https://youtu.be/A-7pTamjBIw?t=917)
**Мысль:** Тяжёлые абзацы заменяются секциями, которые раскрываются, если релевантны человеку (progressive disclosure).

> And the copy itself should never feel heavy. Instead of long, messy paragraphs, break things into sections, but open as you scroll. That way, the page feels light. This is called an accordion. You can set this up in any theme builder and then click it and expands the copy if it's relevant to them.

**Для Напарника:** Объяснения на экране Мир/ориентиры показывать раскрывающимися, а не простынёй.



# 🎬 How to Build Meaningful Animations | Louis Ansa Art Director at makemepulse — awwwards.


### Выразительную анимацию только для главной кнопки  ·  [04:27](https://youtu.be/Npu68jiGG94?t=267)
**Мысль:** Сильный эффект на каждой кнопке — перебор; главный CTA получает богатую анимацию, остальные упрощённую версию того же принципа.

> And it's a bit too much so you shouldn't use those buttons as a main element in your website but you should use it for the most important buttons like by button on a project page or keep me updated button on a teasing page for following product and in that way you can build yaki and this one is the main element and the other buttons are just following the same principle in a simpler way.

**Для Напарника:** Эффект Фон Ресторфа: один заметный отклик у «Начать фокус», вторичные кнопки спокойнее.


### «Сайты-смузи»: все анимации сразу ослабляют сообщение  ·  [05:24](https://youtu.be/Npu68jiGG94?t=324)
**Мысль:** Когда команда пихает в проект каждую найденную анимацию, послание теряется и вводит пользователя в заблуждение.

> And it creates something I call smoothies websites and it's a project where the production team the developers and the designers it was so excited to do an amazing website that they tried to stuff in every animation they could find on the web even if it wasn't really fitting in the project and yeah your message is not as strong when you do this and it's misleading for the user so it's not always the best option.

**Для Напарника:** Согласуется с правилом CLAUDE.md §10: не добавлять glow и анимации без привязки к принципу.


### Подсказка «есть ещё» через направление анимации, без текста  ·  [12:59](https://youtu.be/Npu68jiGG94?t=779)
**Мысль:** Если элементы въезжают справа налево, пользователь интуитивно понимает, что есть ещё контент, без надписи.

> But you can solve this problem just with animation without necessarily writing it so for instance this one you scroll down and when you scroll down you actually show to the users that there is more content for instance if you animate this India in the opposite way meaning from the right to the left the user won't see that there is more content here it is very simple but intuitively knows that there are more things to see.

**Для Напарника:** Лента чата и очередь шагов: показывать, что следующий шаг ждёт, движением карточки, а не подписью.



# 🎬 How to Choose the Best Colors for UI/UX Design | Color Theory, Contrast & Accessibility Tips — Chandini Creates


### Текст через шкалу непрозрачности: 100/80/60/40/20%  ·  [09:30](https://youtu.be/SdWDrFqkax0?t=570)
**Мысль:** Цвет текста с альфой смешивается с фоном, а 100% чёрного выглядит слишком резко.

> When it comes to black and white so these two colors are mostly used for your text colors so instead of making the tonal palette in the similar way what I'm going to do is I'm going to just let me take a copy of this black and let's duplicate this few a few times right your first shade is going to be the black in full opacity and the second one will be black with 80% opacity and then 60 40 and 20 so why we use opacity based colors for text is mostly because opacity based colors tend to blend well with your background so if you use a 100 shade and black color it usually ends up standing out way too much and having a very harsh look to it that is why we go for opacity based color.

**Для Напарника:** Ступени foreground/muted текста Напарника делать как альфа от тёплого белого, но проверять контраст 4.5:1 на фоне.



# 🎬 How To Design Color Accessible Webpages — Stefany Newman - Accessibility Instructor 


### Дизайн в градациях серого: иерархия без опоры на цвет  ·  [11:09](https://youtu.be/BW_PjPR825w?t=669)
**Мысль:** Сначала строишь выделение размером и жирностью в чёрно-белом, потом добавляешь цвет как усиление; если хорошо в сером, в цвете будет лучше.

> If you are a designer and you design a lot of mockups in Adobe Illustrator, what I suggest is try to design your mockups in grayscale. Do not add any color. Just use font size or, you know, boldness to make things stand out, and then add the color. This is what I do in web design too, in web development, like I will create a component and then I will grayscale the page and see how it stands out. And then when I add color, it will just progressively enhance it rather than make it just rely on color exactly. And I know from graphic designers that they sometimes make logos only in grayscale and they say if it looks good in grayscale, it's going to look even better in color.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять экраны Дома и Фокуса в grayscale: иерархия должна читаться без оттенков неба и акцентов.



# 🎬 How To Increase Conversions With Design — Flux Academy


### Фокус: чем меньше кликабельного, тем выше конверсия (паралич выбора)  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/YkxGTwbRQ9w?t=22)
**Мысль:** У Zola десятки кликабельных элементов, у Netflix два (старт, вход). При избытке вариантов люди зависают, ничего не делая; в среднем на сайте 4,39 ссылки.

> The first principle i want you to think about is focus and what do i mean by focus check out this website for zola how many things here you can actually go ahead and click how many things are clickable so there are actually the two call to action button let's go and find a couple there's a bunch of them up in the navigation and there's also at the top start your free registry and then find the couple and help and log in and then below there's all of these four different options to click from there's a lot of things to click about now check out netflix landing page how many things can you click here you can either get started or you can sign in that's it so that is focus is you have a one clear message where you're directing people to exactly the one thing that you want them to do we there's a phenomenon in psychology called paralysis analysis so if you have too many options you're going to start thinking about it and not be sure what should i do and that's going to end up leading to less conversions because you're not going to do anything now here's a super interesting graph that i took from unbalanced unbounded you know platform for landing pages so they have a lot of data about conversions and here's what they say basically the less links you have on your site or the less yeah the in the section that you're looking at the more chances are that it's going to convert so most websites have you know on average 4.39 links and the if you have less than that you're actually going to convert more definitely do not add more than that so that's principle number one focus less choices very clear call to action for one specific thing.

**Для Напарника:** На Доме и лендинге минимум ссылок; на Фокусе одна главная цель на экран.


### Поток: каждая секция со своим фокусом и иерархией ведёт к убеждению  ·  [02:10](https://youtu.be/YkxGTwbRQ9w?t=130)
**Мысль:** Покупку редко делают с первого экрана; нужен маршрут: фокус на CTA, видео, выгоды, сценарии, отзывы, а каждый блок сфокусирован.

> The next thing is flow and what do i mean by flow so a lot of times even if we're putting just one single call to action it's very hard to sell specifically if you're selling something and people need to spend money they're not going to immediately you know they land on your website see a button buy now and they're going to click it right you need to go ahead and convince them that this is the right thing for them so you need to create some kind of a flow to take them through a journey to convince them that this is the right solution for them so let's check out an example here's pandadox you can see at the beginning at the heroes section they do have kind of a focused call to action just like we covered basically it's mostly start free trial they also have a request to demo but that's the main call to action now what happens if i'm not convinced i don't want to start my free trial yet so i'm scrolling the next thing they have is actually a video here that plays around and shows you how the product actually looks like you're keeping on scrolling and now you're getting into kind of a section that go through each of the benefits of the software and shows you more demos and more things that you can do with it and then the next section is hey here are some use cases who is this actually good for so is it good for sales or hr or marketing and so and here are the use cases of what you can do with it so proposals quotes and so forth and so forth now this is how they're building the flow and note that each one of these sections also have a very clear focus just like we talked about in the earlier principle and very clear hierarchy they're not confusing you with too many things they're saying here's the thing to look at this is streamlining your workflow with every document in this section you're only looking at this see how other our customers are using panadoc here this is a section that is testimonials basically what other customers are saying so basically every section has its focus and its hierarchy to clearly take you through the flow of convincing you that this is the right product for you.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг: герой, демо Фокуса, как растёт остров, отзывы, снова CTA; у каждой секции один посыл.


### Внимание ведут размер, цвет и контраст: CTA должен выделяться на фоне  ·  [05:33](https://youtu.be/YkxGTwbRQ9w?t=333)
**Мысль:** В примере лица и крупный заголовок перетягивают взгляд, а кнопка не заметна; контрастный цвет, отсутствующий в фоне, захватывает внимание.

> Designers we're choosing elements we're choosing the size of elements we're choosing the color of elements and that helps to guide the attention of the users on the page so in this example here what grabs our attention here right is this the people looking at the faces of people which is also like psychologically we're all like we like to look at other people's faces is it the big kind of like solana store title by the time that you're you need to see the call to action which in this case is view collection you can see that it does not grab my attention it does not stand out and so something that's very classic is just to use a collar that's very contrasted with what's going on in the background in this example from campaign monitor you can see that they're using a purple color that really stands out from both the background it's contrasted from the background but also other elements on you know the image that they're using is not using purple so it's really easy to grab a tension by using a contrasted color and you should do that as a designer.

**Для Напарника:** Главная кнопка Фокуса — единственный контрастный акцентный цвет на экране, в рамках коридора светлоты фона.



# 🎬 How to MAKE YOUR GAME LOOK GOOD! — Brackeys


### Фокальный элемент: контраст должен быть у одного, а не у всех  ·  [03:21](https://youtu.be/nvbQ9_bzx1k?t=201)
**Мысль:** Если контраст, насыщенность и размер применены ко всему, ничего не выделяется. Приглушив окружение, автор сразу делает героя точкой притяжения взгляда.

> So at this point, it's a good idea to ask ourselves which part of the scene is most important and should be the thing the player sees first. In this case, I would probably say the player character. So let's try making that a focal element. Now there's a few ways of making something a focal element. Some direct influences of focus are high contrast, saturation, size and movement. This of course only works if one of few elements have these. At the moment, there is a lot of contrast in the scene, but it applies to everything. So nothing really stands out. Let's fix that. I will go ahead and make the environment, the coins and the bug stack, to tone down the overall contrast of the scene. And just like that, it looks a lot better and your eye is immediately drawn towards the player. And make him a bit bigger to emphasise the effect even more,.

**Для Напарника:** На экране Фокуса один главный элемент (таймер/кот) должен быть самым контрастным, а остальное приглушено.


### Свет ведёт игрока, а движение показывает, что объект интерактивен  ·  [05:00](https://youtu.be/nvbQ9_bzx1k?t=300)
**Мысль:** Игроки идут к свету, а на статичной сцене любое медленное движение сразу маркирует интерактивные предметы. Свет рассказывает маленькую историю о том, откуда он падает.

> Let's try adding some lighting to the scene. I find that when working with lighting, it helps if I think of it like I'm creating a narrative. In this case, we could imagine that we just wandered into this cave, and now some light is spilling in from the entrance. So I'll add light that shines some light from the entrance onto the box. But the scene still feels a bit unbalanced. There's a lot going on the left side with the contrast and lighting. So I'll add a dim light on the right to balance things out a bit. This also guides the player towards the exit of the level. And this is definitely a key takeaway. Players always go towards the light. Of course, right now the coins are looking a bit off. At first glance, you might not even realise that there are coins you can pick up. So let's fix that. As I mentioned earlier, movement has a huge influence on focus, especially when everything else is static like in the scene. So I've gone ahead and made this little script that makes the coins float up and down. And just like that, they clearly stand out as something you can interact with.

**Для Напарника:** На острове и у костра в AppBackdrop интерактивные находки можно мягко подсвечивать и слегка покачивать (медленно, по правилам диегетичности), чтобы они читались как «можно взять».



# 🎬 How to pick the right colors for your website or app | UI/UX design tips — Rachel How


### Акцентный цвет ставь на то, на что хочешь привлечь внимание  ·  [02:41](https://youtu.be/ewRYw4pnKQU?t=161)
**Мысль:** Акцент на компоненте естественно притягивает взгляд.

> So the guiding principle is if you want people to pay attention on a certain component then it is a great idea to use an accent color on that component itself so people's eyes just naturally drawn to it.

**Для Напарника:** Тёплый акцент применять только к главной кнопке старта и редким находкам, не к декору.


### Мало цветов: если всё приоритет, то ничего не приоритет  ·  [10:35](https://youtu.be/ewRYw4pnKQU?t=635)
**Мысль:** На YouTube цветная только подписка, поиск не лезет в глаза. Слишком много цветов делает всё приоритетным, и человек не знает, куда смотреть.

> Basically with lesser colors on the ui it helps your brand stand out more on youtube which is the interface that you're looking right now they use very minimal color palettes to not drive users away from the call to action so as you can see besides the youtube logo that stands out the search bar isn't right in your face call to action is going to be the subscribe button down here and also maybe the shop now for ads and other youtube videos that they want to recommend you if you use too many colors in your interface then everything becomes a priority and users don't know where to look so it's always great to have minimal colors and let the real call to action stand out more.

**Для Напарника:** На Фокусе держать один цветной элемент (кнопка старта или кольцо), остальное в нейтральных тонах, что важно для СДВГ.



# 🎬 How to Stop AI from Killing Your Critical Thinking | Advait Sarkar | TED — TED


### Линзы и провокации: ИИ подсвечивает значимое для задачи, человек остаётся читателем  ·  [07:34](https://youtu.be/3lPnN8omdPA?t=454)
**Мысль:** Настраиваемые микрорепрезентации текста и комментарии-критика создают гибрид ручного и автоматического чтения.

> Except these aren't really just summaries. We think of them more as lenses. They're customizable micro representations of the text that can emphasize what is most relevant to the task at hand. So in this case, Clara selects the consumer’s lens. She can select a section for deeper reading, in this case the first one. As she reads, she makes notes about her thoughts and highlights excerpts from the document. As she reads, she also sees AI-generated commentary and critiques. We call these provocations. Here's a provocation that raises a potential opportunity, which she highlights and annotates. Note how this process is a hybrid of completely manual reading and completely relying on AI to read for you. Clara still reads, but intentionally and strategically.

**Для Напарника:** Для дробления задачи: показывать шаги в нескольких «линзах» (короткий, подробный), оставляя выбор за человеком.


### Нет чат-бокса: ИИ молча помогает, предлагая альтернативы и контраргументы, а не достраивая идеи  ·  [10:30](https://youtu.be/3lPnN8omdPA?t=630)
**Мысль:** Помощь встроена в рабочее пространство и не имитирует собеседника; провокации усиливают собственный аргумент человека.

> As she writes, she sees provocations that, rather than autocompleting her ideas, they raise alternatives, they identify fallacies, they offer counterarguments to help her strengthen and develop her own argument. There's something you won't find anywhere in this interface. And that's a chat box. Clara’s not having to chat with anything to do her work, yet she is silently and appropriately assisted by her computer as a computer and not as an ersatz human.

**Для Напарника:** Контрапункт к чату Напарника: встраивать подсказки в моменты действия (на шаге, на старте), а не только в ленту.



# 🎬 How to Use Color Theory in Character Design and Like, Actually Know What You're Doing! — Brookes Eggleston - Character Design Forge


### Светлота как инструмент иерархии: глаз идёт к самому светлому  ·  [02:11](https://youtu.be/W2LJAP-Srhk?t=131)
**Мысль:** Значение (светлота) назначает важность: на тёмном фоне светлое пятно мгновенно притягивает взгляд. Иерархия важного рассказывает историю и ведёт взгляд.

> It's good to know how important value is now value is simply how light or dark something is and you can look at something that's completely grayscale like this and choose a value that is closer to light or closer to black but it isn't just in grayscale for example this is a lighter value green and this is a darker value green understanding value is helpful because you can use it to assign importance to something visually for example if you have a lot of dark gray in an image and then something is suddenly white or really light the eye is immediately drawn to that place value gives you the chance to create contrast in your image to a big part of making good art is creating this hierarchy of importance in your image it's a way to tell a story or communicate where you want the eye to be drawn like for example the face might be the most important place so that's where the eye is drawn to first and then a symbol is on the chest and then the arms and maybe least important in this case are the legs so you can emphasize and de-emphasize those things with value.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает коридор светлоты фона (§3): на тёмном фоне главный акцент (кнопка, таймер, лицо кота) делать самым светлым элементом.



# 🎬 How We Feel: Color Use & Abstract Concepts | App Breakdown #14 — App Breakdown


### Выбор эмоции по цвету: сжатие/раскрытие, потому что люди не знают названий чувств  ·  [02:25](https://youtu.be/wau3vQ2jr3Q?t=145)
**Мысль:** Эмоций много, и люди не знают их точных названий, поэтому приложение начинает с цвета (энергия и приятность), а потом раскрывает варианты. Хаптика срабатывает при смене выбранной эмоции.

> Okay, tap the color that best describes how you feel right now. And you're going to see they do a very good job of compressing and expanding emotions to help you actually understand which one you're looking for cuz there are a lot, but people don't actually know their names or how they feel. So, let's go for right now. I am high energy and pleasant. So, I'm going to tap the yellow here. And the moment I tap the yellow, then they all expand. So, now Oh, wow. Okay. So, as I can scroll, this is sort of like the Apple Watch UI. Like on the Apple Watch, you have the little apps going around here. It's sort of the same thing. And the app that's in the Centroid expands shows at the bottom and you can tap it. And also there's a haptic every time a new emotion is selected and you can scroll like this like it's pretty cool.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-ин состояния в Доме: начинать с простого выбора (цвет/чип «как сейчас»), а не со списка слов. Хаптика при реальной смене значения (hapticThreshold) совпадает с принципом из CLAUDE.md.


### Shared-element transition: элемент остаётся, остальное приходит вокруг  ·  [03:44](https://youtu.be/wau3vQ2jr3Q?t=224)
**Мысль:** Иллюстрация выбранной эмоции переносится на следующий экран, а остальная информация появляется сверху и снизу. Постоянство объекта делает переход понятным.

> So they keep the element, they keep the little animation, not the animation, the little illustration, bring it up, and then have the rest of the information come down even though they don't keep the text.

**Для Напарника:** Переходы setup→starting→running в Фокусе: ключевой элемент (кот/кольцо таймера) остаётся на месте, остальные меняются вокруг него.


### Пустые состояния должны давать ценность с первого дня; статистика не должна быть однообразной  ·  [15:07](https://youtu.be/wau3vQ2jr3Q?t=907)
**Мысль:** Статистика без данных скучна; нужно, чтобы с первого дня было ощущение пользы, а визуализации выглядели по-разному, а не как одинаковые карточки.

> I wish they had slightly better empty states so that you get some value from day one, but nonetheless, very nice. This is the kind of thing that I was talking about when we reviewed or when we broke down all trails where they had a page showing your stats, but all the stats essentially looked the same and it was not exciting. This is exciting.

**Для Напарника:** Пустое состояние Мира/Дома: «Плана на сегодня нет — и это ноль, не минус». Показывать остров с первого дня, а не пустую сетку.


### Крупное поле ввода снижает ощущение, что нужно много писать  ·  [16:05](https://youtu.be/wau3vQ2jr3Q?t=965)
**Мысль:** Большой текст в поле ввода делает так, что человек не чувствует объёма написанного, а экран остаётся чистым.

> Something that I don't see a lot of but it's very cool here is the text that you're typing into the chat or into the modles is very large. So it's basically made for you to not have to write a lot or not feel like you're writing a lot and it's just very clean.

**Для Напарника:** Поле ввода шага/чата крупным шрифтом и с одним полем, чтобы короткая запись ощущалась как достаточная.



# 🎬 I Realized My Game’s Rewards Were Boring — RookieDev


### Разная подача для обычных и редких наград, иначе ничто не выглядит важным  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/sW2U1_2_zNE?t=434)
**Мысль:** Если всё подаётся одинаково, игрок учится, что ничто не особенно важно. Обычное улучшение быстрое, открытие новой сущности и вехи заслуживают большей подачи.

> But right now, I think some rewards that should feel important don't feel different enough from the normal stuff. A normal upgrade can be fast. Click, number changes, done. But the first time you find a discovery, maybe that deserves more. A major ascension milestone, probably more. Something rare, definitely more. I need a clearer difference between, "Cool, you bought another level." And, "Wait, you just unlock something new." Because if everything gets the same presentation, the player learns that nothing is especially important.

**Для Напарника:** Ввести градацию подачи: обычная сессия - тихий рост, редкая находка или веха - полная хореография со звуком и хаптикой.



# 🎬 I redesigned IRCTC App! - Product Design | Ansh Mehra — The Cutting Edge School


### Определи основное действие и отдай ему максимум места  ·  [01:43](https://youtu.be/2f57yfJPmQA?t=103)
**Мысль:** Когда на экране много элементов, глаз не знает, куда смотреть; нужно понять цель экрана и дать главному действию максимум площади, всё остальное по возможности вырезать.

> Important thing here is to decide what is the primary action what is the common use case of this screen the most common use case is obviously to book a ticket when you're traveling there are two kinds of travel there's a one-way trip and there's a round trip and I have not used this mobile experience before so I'm still pretty confused as to how I can book just a round trip versus just a one-way trip secondly because there are so many items here so much Tech so many images my eyes don't really know where to look so I think the first principle here is to understand what is the core objective of the screen and then understand how can we give maximum real estate to that one primary action without adding elements that can distract you now in design there's a very basic principle that if you can get rid of something cut it away anything that comes on the screen should be absolutely necessary if you can cut away any word any single line of text that is recommended.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус: основное действие «начать» должно доминировать; вторичное убрать или раскрывать (progressive disclosure).


### Иерархия поля ввода: размер и цвет, мелкая подпись и крупное значение  ·  [04:26](https://youtu.be/2f57yfJPmQA?t=266)
**Мысль:** В поле ввода подпись («From») мелкая, значение («New Delhi») крупное; цвет и размер вместе строят иерархию.

> A very core principle of creating components is visual hierarchy if I am making an input field I need to make sure that from is in a very small font and New Delhi is in a Big Font both the color and the size establish your hierarchy.

**Для Напарника:** В формах Дома/Фокуса (шаг, длительность) значение крупнее и контрастнее подписи.


### Иконки и подписи не всегда нужны, если смысл очевиден  ·  [07:33](https://youtu.be/2f57yfJPmQA?t=453)
**Мысль:** Дата очевидно датой, ей не нужна иконка календаря; подпись нужна, когда непонятно, что за данные; иконки полезны в длинных списках.

> You don't always need these icons if I remove Journey date if I remove this calendar icon your eyes will know that 12th June is the date itself you need labels when it is not very obvious what this data is for so if this wasn't 12 June 2023 if it was a normal text then I need a label because otherwise somebody would not even know what is this for is this from is this two is this your name is this your house address how would you know but when it comes to time and when it comes to date somebody can literally see and guess that this is a date so you don't need the label icons are usually helpful when you have a very long list but in this case even if you cut off these icons it would not affect the user experience overall.

**Для Напарника:** Убирать избыточные иконки на карточках Дома и Фокуса, оставлять там, где смысл неочевиден.


### Слои поверхности: фон серый, карточка светлее, поле ввода серое  ·  [08:31](https://youtu.be/2f57yfJPmQA?t=511)
**Мысль:** Чем ближе к пользователю, тем светлее карточка; серое поле внутри белой карточки читается как «сюда можно вводить» по привычке из других приложений.

> Towards the content and notice that in terms of hierarchy I have a gray surface on top of that you have a white card so as you move towards the screen the card should increase in lightness so all cards are in white so anything that you see in white is placed on that hierarchy and then the blue CTA is on top of that card but when it comes to the input field if you put something in Gray then it feels like you can fill something in it feels a layer under that white card so it's not like I made this into a gray and then the input feels white keeping it gray makes it feel like you can type in it now there is no explicit logic behind it's just that because we have been using so many apps this is the kind of behavior that we would expect when we're seeing something like this.

**Для Напарника:** Слои на светлоте: сохранять коридор 0.152–0.172 фона, вводить различие слоёв оттенком и насыщенностью, а не яркостью.


### Серая иконка рядом с текстом сигнализирует «кликабельно»  ·  [10:30](https://youtu.be/2f57yfJPmQA?t=630)
**Мысль:** Без значка строка воспринимается как простой список, а не как действие.

> Next to the text so that you know that it is clickable otherwise if you don't put an icon people will not know that it is clickable they will think that it is simply a list.

**Для Напарника:** Тапабельные строки Дома/Мира снабжать намёком кликабельности (шеврон/иконка).



# 🎬 I Redesigned the ENTIRE Spotify UI from Scratch — Juxtopposed


### Различать типы элементов формой и деталями, а не только обложкой  ·  [01:06](https://youtu.be/suhEIUapSJQ?t=66)
**Мысль:** Если убрать обложку, альбом, плейлист и подкаст неразличимы; мы привыкаем узнавать вещи по раскладке и форме, а не по иконкам и названиям.

> List of songs it has albums playlists and recently podcasts and audiobooks also more on these later but the thing is if I remove the cover image would you be able to tell what's what at the first glance yeah that's a problem we get used to the way different things look like on apps and not just through icons or titles but the layouts and shapes so if we had little details on these items that would be much easier to distinguish anywhere on the app.

**Для Напарника:** Карточки шагов, ориентиров острова и наград различать формой и мелкими деталями, а не только цветом или текстом.


### Профиль и настройки далеко от большого пальца  ·  [02:23](https://youtu.be/suhEIUapSJQ?t=143)
**Мысль:** Настройки спрятаны в верхнем углу, куда не достаёт палец; критика предлагает вынести их вниз.

> On the other hand your profile settings and everything is hidden up here far away from any point your thumb can reach instead it could have been down here merged with the whole subscription.

**Для Напарника:** Важные действия Напарника (начать фокус, ответить коту) держать в зоне большого пальца в нижней части экрана (нижний таб-бар AppNav уже в этой зоне).


### Главная должна «ощущаться как дом»: пользовательский контроль над рядами  ·  [07:15](https://youtu.be/suhEIUapSJQ?t=435)
**Мысль:** Рекомендации, не связанные с человеком, и подкасты, навязанные с самого начала, разрушают чувство дома; автор предлагает скрывать, закреплять и добавлять разделы.

> Yeah the homepage is supposed to be a marketing funnel but history has shown that we music listeners are very committed to our preferences and this page might be called home but it doesn't feel like home first of all let's switch all the items to the new style that properly differentiates them on desktop you can't see all the items on the rows you got to go to another page for that but it's not like there's so many That Couldn't just be on the main page with one scroll to the right like that's the most expected Behavior here then you get all these recommendations of podcasts and audiobooks right from the beginning I don't know how it's recommending me these specific things but sure you do your marketing it's just that this is the homepage which means every user should be able to customize what they want to see here so to fix that I'm adding an option to hide a section complete or if you like it too much just pin it to the top actually why not let you add or remove whatever you like to your own homepage and many you like this could help you pin move or hide different rows or if you want add a new row from your library or turn Dynamic recommendation rows on or off your homepage your rules.

**Для Напарника:** Дом Напарника: показывать только то, что нужно человеку сейчас, давать убрать лишнее; принцип «один вопрос за визит».


### Быстрое «сохранить на потом» вместо создания плейлиста  ·  [09:06](https://youtu.be/suhEIUapSJQ?t=546)
**Мысль:** Для вовлечения взаимодействие должно быть мгновенным; нужно место для отложенного, чтобы не заставлять организовывать.

> Expecting people to create their own playlists to save things for later is not the best way to draw engagement either cuz the interactions have to be quick right so we need a place like saves to save something to explore later.

**Для Напарника:** Быстрый захват мысли/задачи «на потом» одним касанием на Доме, без обязательной формы или планирования.


### Закрытие панели не должно требовать выхода из зоны одной руки  ·  [11:31](https://youtu.be/suhEIUapSJQ?t=691)
**Мысль:** Два касания, чтобы открыть, и невозможность закрыть свайпом заставляют тянуться; лишние шаги в неудобной зоне раздражают.

> To interact with it like you got to go down here to see the lyrics then click on here to expand the lyrics that's two interactions only for it not to close when you drag it down so you got to get out of your comfy one-handed Zone to close the lyrics.

**Для Напарника:** Модальные панели Дома и Фокуса: закрытие свайпом вниз и кнопкой в нижней зоне; не требовать верхних углов.


### Динамический цвет панели показывает состояние: играет или на паузе  ·  [14:19](https://youtu.be/suhEIUapSJQ?t=859)
**Мысль:** Цвет, который живёт, пока идёт воспроизведение, и серый на паузе, сообщает состояние без текста.

> Last but not least this Bottom bar really needs some love first of all it's just not giving a Spotify with that black soulless background how about making it with a dynamic color that shows as long as you're playing the song and goes gray when you pause.

**Для Напарника:** Мини-индикатор активной сессии Фокуса в таб-баре: тёплый, пока сессия идёт, нейтральный в паузе (событие, не пульсация).



# 🎬 I Studied 1000 Landing Pages, Here's What Works in 2026 — Arsh Sanwarwala | ThrillX


### Длинная страница не вредит, вредит плохая структура: страница как продавец  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/PcwuZJNJny4?t=434)
**Мысль:** Холодный посетитель ничего не знает; страница должна последовательно давать ценность, доказательства и снимать возражения.

> Lesson number three is that a long landing page does not kill conversions. Bad structure does. There is so much debate online about what the perfect length for a landing page looks like and countless studies online claiming one thing or the other. I'm here to tell you that there is nothing wrong with a long landing page as long as every section is adding value and getting people one step closer to conversion. I think people tend to forget, right? Long gone are the days with Google ads and Facebook ads that you can just throw up an ad and you'll start generating leads. There's no world in today's day and age where someone who knows nothing about your business, nothing about your brand, and nothing about your offer is just going to magically click on your ad and convert straight away. The ad is simply just to get the click. At the end of the day, your landing page is what needs to do the selling. And you need to treat your landing page like it's a saleserson. This means you need to have multiple value props, right? Multiple social proof sections, multiple sections where you're addressing people's fear, uncertainty, objections, and multiple sections where you're explaining to the customer why you're the absolute best fit and best solution for their problem to get them over the edge to convert.

**Для Напарника:** Лендинг Напарника может быть длинным, если каждая секция (польза, доказательство, возражение) приближает к первому «Фокусу».


### Люди читают 20%, остальное сканируют: структура, заголовки и «думай как читатель»  ·  [09:17](https://youtu.be/PcwuZJNJny4?t=557)
**Мысль:** Заголовки, подзаголовки, буллеты и жирный текст помогают найти ответ; в заголовке уже должна быть выгода.

> That people don't read, they scan. People will only read 20% of the content on your website. The other 80% they just scan. So people are just skimming your page and are trying to look for answers quickly and efficiently. So the question is, how do you make sure that people get the information they need without getting lost in the noise? It starts with structuring your landing pages in a way that's easy to scan. You need to utilize things like headings and subheadings. Bullet points to highlight the main value props like these examples here and bolding text to emphasize certain items, especially for your headlines. Right? This is something I see brands messing up all of the time. They use generic headlines like here's what we do, what makes us unique, how it works, or our services. Because of the fact that people don't read, they scan. You need to operate under the framework that somebody will only read the headlines on your landing page alone. Just imagine they don't even read any of the body text on your page. Is the benefit clear and communicative in just the headline alone. So instead of what makes us unique, just simply say what makes you unique in the headline itself so that they can quickly scan it. The key here is to think like a reader, not a writer. What's the one thing that people should know at a glance? put that front and center.

**Для Напарника:** Экраны приложения: короткие реплики, главное действие выделено, второстепенное приглушено.


### Визуальная иерархия и «меньше — лучше»: лишний выбор ведёт к параличу  ·  [12:48](https://youtu.be/PcwuZJNJny4?t=768)
**Мысль:** Шрифт, размер, вес, цвет ведут взгляд от главного к второстепенному; слишком много кнопок и ссылок вызывает analysis paralysis; исправленная иерархия дала +64%.

> Lesson number six is that visual hierarchy is your best friend. This is basically the art of guiding a user's attention and the flow of information from the most important elements to the least important. I always like to use this graphic when explaining it to clients. Using things like font, size, weight, color, etc., you can guide people's attentions to where you want it to go from the most prioritized elements to the least prioritized. Here's examples of what not to do with these poorly put together landing pages where, you know, there's so many elements on the page that I have no idea where to look at and what to do first and where my eye should go first. There's no clear distinction between body text, between headlines, between CT buttons. It's all over the place. The other issue is that there's way too much on the page. I always like to go by the mantra that less is more. In other words, when you give people too much choice, it equals no choice at all. This is especially true when it comes to landing pages and design, right? When you give people way too many options, too many buttons, too many texts, too many links, too many things that they can do, they're going to enter a state of analysis paralysis where they won't know what to do next, and they're most likely just going to completely exit your page. Here's a real life example of this in action. In the before version for a client, they didn't have any visual hierarchy. the red clashed with one another. The headline was subdued. It wasn't prominent on the page. It didn't stand out and there was no sense of attention. Everything was kind of competing for attention at once. In the after version, we fixed all of this and created a clear flow of information from the headline to the sub headline to the CTA button to create that sense of hierarchy. And what we saw was a 64% lift in the conversion rate.

**Для Напарника:** На Доме и в Фокусе: один главный элемент, явный порядок заголовок, пояснение, кнопка; убрать лишние ссылки.



# 🎬 If You Don’t Understand Psychology, You Don’t Understand Web Design — Sam Crawford | Web Design Expert


### Закон Фитса на телефоне: главное действие большое и в зоне большого пальца  ·  [02:51](https://youtu.be/J7LWfqWbx1o?t=171)
**Мысль:** Время достижения цели зависит от размера и расстояния; на мобильном под одним пальцем цена умножается; главное действие должно быть самым лёгким физически.

> So, Fitts's law says that the time it takes someone to reach a target depends on two things: how big the target is and then how far away it is. So, the bigger and closer something is, the faster and more confidently a person will move towards it. And inversely, the smaller and the farther away it is, the more effort it takes. And effort is where people inevitably just give up. On a website, this is the difference between a call to action somebody actually clicks on without having to think and one that they have to aim at. A small button floating in an awkward corner surrounded by other small links is a target that the brain has to work to hit. On a phone, where the whole thing is happening under one moving thumb, the cost of this multiplies massively. Watch how the best mobile experiences handle this. The main action is large. It sits low on the screen inside the natural arc of your thumb. It's not crowded by, you know, 10 competing links at the same size. The single most important thing you want someone to do is also the easiest thing on the page to physically do. So, go and look at your primary call to action button right now on a phone held in one hand. If your thumb has to stretch to the top of the screen or hunt between cramped links, then you're adding friction at the exact moment you're asking someone to take action. So, >> >> make the important target big and bring it into reach.

**Для Напарника:** Кнопку «Начать фокус» держать крупной и низко на экране, без 10 конкурирующих ссылок рядом.

> This is why when people talk about Aesthetics and learning design it's not just I want to make it look pretty because that's what I like to do or because I want to show off Aesthetics matter in that they can help reduce extraneous cognitive load now there is a limit to that and a Tipping Point where we can very easily Focus too much on making things look impressive and Grand and actually go from reducing to increasing extraneous load by putting so much stuff on the screen it starts to get confusing remember every decision we make in terms of modality in terms of navigation in terms of how content is delivered whether it's digital or human or some combination of the two is all about helping the learner succeed if that is not the reason you're adding something you need to really stop and question yourself hard why is this thing being added if it's just for you if it's to make yourself look clever or to show off or to make it pretty simply for the sake of that please reconsider you are doing the exact opposite of what you think you are doing you are hindering the learner by increasing extraneous loads.

example of google so if you actually go on google.com the only thing that you see like primarily centered on the page are just some search options you basically have an input field you can type something in there and then you can basically hit enter or search so as you can see they've actually reduced all a lot of cognitive load from the user and it allows users to actually just get right into the gist of searching for things once a user has searched for example if i search for nasa i have some of the other options as well i can obviously see some of the results i can actually search for images news articles videos so on and so forth so all of these other options weren't really presented on the previous one i know some of them were like images etc but not all of them so again # 🎬 Mechanically Speaking: Shooting in First Person - [game array] — Mason C Miller


### Физические ограничения и внешний вид, отражающий функцию  ·  [11:34](https://youtu.be/13iMO9s-q0c?t=694)
**Мысль:** Скрытая математика незаметна игроку; ясные физические ограничения (обойма, скорость) и внешний вид, голос и анимации оружия сообщают его роль до использования.

> The first step is to avoid balancing weapons with theoretical weaknesses that might not be clear. Instead use physical limitations. Physical limitations are tangible and easy to understand. Like the number of bullets in a clip. The rate of fire. Or the velocity of the bullet. Theoretical weaknesses are, by their nature, invisible to the player. They’re hidden math. Maybe a weapon does slightly less damage per bullet, or its accuracy is a few percentages better or worse than another’s. These theoretical characteristics will never be as clearly communicated to the player as physical ones. We want players to act with intentionality. For that to happen, we need each weapon’s appearance to reflect its function, so that the players know how each one works before they even pick it up. This is especially important to sci fi shooters where alien guns might behave differently than the ones we have on Planet Earth. If we make the gun small and slim, then we’ll communicate that it’s rapid fire with weak damage. If it’s big and bulky then that’ll say that it’s slow but powerful. Pay attention to its animations. The way it reloads. The way you carry it. The way you equip it. Then there’s the reticle. It might look like a sniper rifle’s, small and focused or a rocket launcher’s, big and inaccurate. What about the gun’s voice. Is it soft. Or is it loud?

**Для Напарника:** Каждый вариант выбора в Напарнике должен визуально и звуково сообщать, что он делает, ещё до тапа.

# 🎬 My App Design Process: What Took Me 5 Years to Learn in 10 Minutes — Chris Raroque


### Отбрасывать красивое, если оно не служит функции  ·  [04:18](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=258)
**Мысль:** Карточка с глубиной не добавляла смысла (глубина должна выделять); группировка по типу ломала порядок релевантности. Скучный классический список дал лучший UX.

> The first prototype was this concept where the results are inside of a card and the card looked really cool once I physically saw it here mocked up I realized it kind of added some unnecessary depth to the app usually depth is added to highlight or Elevate something for example if we added depth to the search bar it would make that more prominent in the UI but in this case the depth being added to the search results didn't really add anything in my opinion so I know we got this inspiration from this mockup from Airbnb but it makes a lot more sense for them because they have this map in the background so they kind of want to show this and let things float over it but since we just have a white screen in the back and we don't really have anything behind it felt a little bit unnecessary so we decided to not go with that option so another concept was grouping the results by type so if the matches are from the task title they'd be grouped here if they're from the notes they'll be grouped here that made a lot of sense and from a UI perspective it really did feel like this would make the design a little bit more clean but one of the issues with this when we thought about it a little more is the way that my backend and type sense which is the service I'm using for search they return the results and it's ranked by relevancy so the order actually matters if we group things I realize it would actually impact that order let me show you guys an example in the ungroup version it could appear as the second result however in the grouped version it appears as result number four so because there would be an actual impact on functionality tried not to go with the grouping option either and the last concept that we tried was a more traditional search list that you see in a bunch of different other apps it is a little bit more boring compared to the first two but I felt like it had the best ux out of all the three options.

**Для Напарника:** Перед добавлением глубины/свечения/группировки спрашивать, что это выделяет; привязка к принципу из §10 CLAUDE.md.

was really good in theory but when I was using it didn't seem that helpful I was kind of just ignoring the icons every time I ran a search so we decided to cut it because my philosophy is if something isn't adding to the experience it's best to just remove it so it reduces cognitive load on the users but then removing it the design felt super text heavy and I really loved how in these mockups there are icons on the left because it does break up the text a little bit and make it a little bit more breathable so we did try to brainstorm something to replace those icons luckily we realized we forgot to add the complete and incomplete indicators on the task like the literal checkbox that shows that a task was complete maybe we can just put those there and when we tried that I actually feel like the ux improved a little bit because it made searching for incomplete task a little bit easier it's just easier to scan and see oh these ones are incomplete so that was an unintended side effect that came out of that where I feel like the ux actually improved for some reason we just didn't think about doing this in the original design so that was a pretty cool thing that happened so on that note of looking for things to cut and simplifying the UI I was trying to look for more things that weren't that necessary for me or I was ignoring and one of the things I noticed is there's a couple of indicators on the task that I didn't feel like were relevant during the search process one example is this indicator on the task shows how much time was spent on the task this is really relevant on certain screens like the task list screen as I was searching and I was trying to put myself in the user shoes I felt like that task indicator wasn't that relevant to the search so for now we cut all the indicators except for label subtask and Note and I feel like that would cover most of what people are going to be looking for when conducting a search if people ask to bring back the indicators I'll reconsider it but for now we cut it just to keep things simple.
